X
, so we have a functor

(15.6)

\text{ho}:\infty\text{Bio}\longrightarrow\text{Bio}

When
X=N\mathscr{P}
we get

\begin{array}[h]{l}\text{ho}(N\mathscr{P})^{0}=(N\mathscr{P})_{I}=\text{Bio}(I,\mathscr{P})=\mathscr{P}^{0}\;\;,\;\;\text{and for $c_{0},c_{1},\cdots c_{n}\in\mathscr{P}^{0}$}\\
\\
\text{ho}(N\mathscr{P})^{-}(c_{0};c_{1}\cdots c_{n})=\left\{f\in\text{Bio}\left(C_{n}^{-},\mathscr{P}\right),f(e_{i})=c_{i}\right\}/_{\approx}=\mathscr{P}^{-}(c_{0};c_{1},\cdots c_{n}),\end{array}

the homotopy relation reduces to identity, and

(15.7)

\text{ho}(N\mathscr{P})\equiv\mathscr{P}

For a general
X\in\infty\text{Bio}
, and
\varphi\in\text{bSet}(X,N\mathscr{P})
,

\mathscr{P}\in\text{Bio}
, we get the adjoint map of bios

(15.8)

\text{ho}(\varphi):\text{ho}(X)\longrightarrow\text{ho}(N\mathscr{P})\equiv\mathscr{P}.

16
Normal monomorphisms

The category Bit, like the dendroidal category of trees
\Omega
, and unlike the
simplicial category
\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta
, has automorphisms and so the
cofibrations
of

b
Set
f:X\to Y
are defined to be the monomorphisms that are
normal
: for each

G\in\text{Bit}
the group
\text{Aut}(G)
acts freely on
Y_{G}\setminus f(X_{G})
. An
object
X\in\text{bSet}
is
cofibrant
or
normal
if
\phi\to X
is
normal, i.e. for each
G\in\text{Bit}
the group
\text{Aut}(G)
acts freely on

X_{G}
; e.g. every representable
\mathscr{Y}_{G}
is normal since

\mathscr{S}_{G}=\sum\mathscr{S}_{\overline{G}}
has free
S_{n}
-actions. For
X\in\text{bSet}
, let
\text{Sk}_{n}(X)\subseteq X
the sub-
bSet

(16.1)

\text{Sk}_{n}(X)_{G}:=\left\{x\in X_{G},x=\alpha^{\ast}(\overline{x}),\alpha:G\to H,\#H^{1}\leq n\right\}

generated by biticies coming from bits
H
with at most
n

edges
. We obtain the
skeltal filteration

(16.2)

\text{Sk}_{1}(X)\subseteq\text{Sk}_{2}(X)\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\text{Sk}_{n}(X)\subseteq\cdots\bigcup_{n}\text{Sk}_{n}(X)\equiv X

Similarly, for a normal mono
f:X\hookrightarrow Y
define

(16.3)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\text{Sk}_{n}(Y,X):=X\cup\text{Sk}_{n}(Y)\\
\\
X=\text{Sk}_{0}(Y,X)\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\text{Sk}_{n}(Y,X)\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\bigcup_{n}\text{Sk}_{n}(Y,X)\equiv Y\end{array}

We get push out squares

\coprod\limits_{G,x}\partial\mathscr{Y}_{G}
\text{Sk}_{n-1}(Y,X)
\text{Sk}_{n}(Y,X)
\coprod\limits_{G,x}\mathscr{Y}_{G}

the sum taken over isomorphism classes of
(G,x)
,
G\in\text{Bit}
,
x\in Y_{G}

non-degenerate and not in the image of
X_{G}
.

Similarly, a normal
X\in\text{bSet}
can be represented as a union of the

\text{Sk}_{n}(X)
that are given by push-outs

\coprod\limits_{G,x}\partial\mathscr{Y}_{G}
\text{Sk}_{n-1}(X)
\text{Sk}_{n}(X)
\coprod\limits_{G,x}\mathscr{Y}_{G}

the sum taken over isomorphism classes of
(G,x)
,
G\in\text{Bit}
,
x\in X_{G}^{n.d.}\cdot
,
\#G^{1}=n
. It follows that the set of cofibraions of

bSet

(16.6)

\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}:=\left\{f\in\text{bSet}(X,Y),f\;\text{normal mono}\right\}

is closed under composition, push-outs, sums, retracts, and sequential limits.
Moreover, the push-out squares (
) show that
\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}
is the
smallest class that is closed under pushouts, compositions and sequential limits and
contains the generating cofibraions,

(16.7)

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}=\left\{\partial\mathscr{Y}_{G}\;\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.92 0 L 38.82 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.87 C 3.67 3.87 6.64 3.01 6.64 1.94 C 6.64 0.87 3.67 0 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{28.25276pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;\mathscr{Y}_{G}\right\}_{G\in\text{Bit}}.

The small object argument gives a factorization of any map
f\in b\text{Set(X,Y)}
as

f=p\circ j
, with
j
a normal mono and
p
having RLP (-right lifting property) with
respect to all normal monos (
p
a “trivial fibration”). For
f:\phi\to X
we get that
every
X\in\text{bSet}
has a normalization

(16.8)

p:\tilde{X}\to X

We have

(16.9)

\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}\equiv\text{\phantom{1}}^{\perp}(\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}^{\perp})

where
\mathcal{C}^{\perp}
(resp.
\text{\phantom{}}^{\perp}\mathcal{C}
) is the set of
arrows satisfying the right (resp. left) lifting property with respect to

\mathcal{C}
. The inclusion
\subseteq
is tautological; for the converse
\supseteq
use
the retract argument: for

f\in\text{\phantom{}}^{\perp}\left(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\text{bSet}}\right)
, factor

f=p\circ j
with
p\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}^{\perp}
, so there is a lifting

(16.10)

j
f
p
X
Y^{\prime}
Y
Y

so
f
is a retract of
j\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}
, and so

f\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}
too.

Given normal monos
f:A\to X
,
g:B\to Y
, the
pushout-product

(16.11)

\left\{f\boxtimes g\;:\;A\otimes Y\coprod\limits_{A\otimes B}X\otimes B\longrightarrow X\otimes Y\right\}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}

is again a normal mono; this follows from the generaing case of boundary inclusions

f:\partial\mathscr{Y}_{F}\to\mathscr{Y}_{F}
,
g:\partial\mathscr{Y}_{G}\to\mathscr{Y}_{G}
,
F,G\in\text{Bit}
, which
reduce to
\left(\partial\mathscr{Y}_{F}\otimes\mathscr{Y}_{G}\right)\cap\left(\mathscr{Y}_{F}\otimes\partial\mathscr{Y}_{G}\right)=\partial\mathscr{Y}_{F}\otimes\partial\mathscr{Y}_{G}
(c.f.

[
40
, 4.26 (ii)]
).

Similarly denoting by
\mathscr{A}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}
the sturation of the inner
(composition or action) faces, the “
anodyne extensions
” (the “via” of

[
66
]
), for
f\in\mathscr{A}
,
g\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}
, we have
f\boxtimes g\in\mathscr{A}
is again anodyne; this follows from the generating cases

f:\Lambda^{e}\mathscr{Y}_{F}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{Y}_{F},\,e
a composition (resp action)

\text{edge}(s)
of
F
, and
g:\partial\mathscr{Y}_{G}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{Y}_{G}

(c.f.

[
66
, 6.3]
).

It follows by adjunction that we have “Joyal’s exponential property“: For
X(=\mathop{\text{colim}}\limits_{\rightarrow}\text{Sk}_{n}(X))
normal,
\mathscr{Q}\in\infty\text{Bio}
every map

\Lambda^{e}\mathscr{Y}_{G}\otimes X\to\mathscr{Q}
extends to
\mathscr{Y}_{G}\otimes X\to\mathscr{Q}
, so have

\mathscr{Q}^{X}\in\infty\text{Bio}
. Similarly, for a normal mono
f:X\hookrightarrow Y
, with

X,Y
normal, and any
\mathscr{Q}\in\infty\text{Bio}
, the map
f^{\ast}:\mathscr{Q}^{Y}\to\mathscr{Q}^{X}
is an inner fibration between
\infty\text{Bio}
; it is actually a Kan
fibration between the underlying
\infty
categories (cf.
[
66
, 7.2]
)!

17
Quillen Model structure. Ein Märchen

Notation:
We shall write
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})
as a short hand for
”
\mathscr{P}^{-}(c_{0};c_{1},\cdots,c_{n})
and
\mathscr{P}^{+}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{n};c_{0})
“.

The category
\text{sBio}_{C}
, of simplicial bios with a fixes set of objects
C

(and identify maps on
C
), has the
projective
model structure, where a map

f:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{Q}
is a weak equivalence (resp. fibration) iff for all

c_{0},\overline{c}=c_{1}\cdots c_{n}\in C
,
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})\to\mathscr{Q}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})
are weak equivalences (resp. fibrations) in

s\text{Set}
.

There is an equivalent
Reedy
model structure, with the same weak-equivalences,
and fibrations
f:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{Q}
are maps such that for all
c_{0};\overline{c}=c_{1},\cdots,c_{n}\in C
the maps

(17.1)

\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})\longrightarrow\widecheck{\mathscr{P}}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})\prod\limits_{\widecheck{\mathscr{Q}}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})}\mathscr{Q}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})

are fibration in
s\text{Set}
, where

(17.2)

\widecheck{\mathscr{P}}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c}):=\lim\limits_{I\subsetneqq\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\}}\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};c_{I})\quad,\quad c_{I}=c_{i_{1}},\cdots c_{i_{k}}\;\text{for}\;I=\left\{i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}\right\}

For
X\in s\text{Set}
and
c_{0};\overline{c}=c_{1},\cdots,c_{n}\in C
, we have the
”Corollas“ simplicial bios (in fact ordinary simplicial operads!)
C^{-}_{c_{0};c_{1}\ldots c_{n}}(X)
and
C^{+}_{c_{0}\ldots c_{n};c_{0}}(X)
, to be denoted commonely by

C^{\pm}_{c_{0};c_{1}\ldots c_{n}}(X)
, satisfying adjunction

(17.3)

s\text{Bios}_{C}\left(C^{\pm}_{c_{0};\overline{c}}(X),\mathscr{P}\right)\equiv s\text{Set}\left(X,\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})\right)

Similarly we have the simplicial bios

(17.4)

\widecheck{C}^{\pm}_{c_{0};\overline{c}}(X):=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{colim}}}\limits_{I\subsetneqq\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\}}C^{\pm}_{c_{0};c_{I}}(X)

so that

(17.5)

\text{sBio}_{C}\left(\widecheck{C}^{\pm}_{c_{0},\overline{c}},(X),\mathscr{P}\right)\equiv s\text{Set}\left(X,\widecheck{\mathscr{P}}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})\right)

The Reedy model structure is cofibrantly generated, with (resp. trivial) fibrations given
by

(17.6)

\widecheck{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{sBio}_{\scriptstyle c}}\equiv\left\{C_{c_{0},\overline{c}}^{\pm}\left(\Lambda_{m}^{e}\right)\coprod\limits_{\widecheck{C}^{\pm}_{c_{0},\overline{c}}(\Lambda_{m}^{e})}\widecheck{C}_{c_{0},\overline{c}}^{\pm}\left(\Delta(m)\right)\to C^{\pm}_{c_{0},\overline{c}}\left(\Delta(m)\right)\right\}^{\perp}

resp.

(17.7)

\mathcal{W}_{\text{sBio}_{\scriptstyle c}}\cap\widecheck{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{sBio}_{\scriptstyle c}}\equiv\left\{C_{c_{0},\overline{c}}^{\pm}\left(\partial\Delta(m)\right)\coprod\limits_{\widecheck{C}^{\pm}_{c_{0},\overline{c}}(\partial\Delta(m))}\widecheck{C}_{c_{0},\overline{c}}^{\pm}\left(\Delta(m)\right)\to C^{\pm}_{c_{0},\overline{c}}\left(\Delta(m)\right)\right\}^{\perp}

The main lemma here is that for
X\subseteq Y
, and for
c_{0}\,\overline{c}=c_{1}\cdots c_{n}\in C
, the push-out

(17.8)

C^{\pm}_{c_{0};\overline{c}}(X)\coprod\limits_{\widecheck{C}^{\pm}_{c_{0};\overline{c}}(X)}\widecheck{C}^{\pm}_{c_{0};\overline{c}}(Y)
C^{\pm}_{c_{0};\overline{c}}(Y)
\mathscr{Q}
\mathscr{P}
f

gives a weak-equivalence
\left\{f:\mathscr{P}\rightarrow\mathscr{Q}\right\}\in\mathcal{W}_{\text{sBio}_{C}}
.

The category of
open
simplicial bio

\text{sBio}^{\phi}
(so for
\mathscr{P}\in\text{sBio}^{\phi}
,
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\phi)=\phi
), has the cofibrantly generated projective model structure with fibrations

(17.9)

\mathcal{F}_{\text{sBio}^{\phi}}=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{ll}f:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{Q},\left\{\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})\to\mathscr{Q}^{\pm}\left(f^{0}(c_{0});f^{0}(\overline{c})\right)\right\}\in\mathcal{F}_{s\text{Set}}\\
\\
\text{all}\;c_{0},\overline{c}=c_{1}\cdots c_{n}\in\mathscr{P}^{0}\;\text{and}\;\left\{\pi_{0}i^{\ast}j^{\ast}\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\pi_{0}i^{\ast}j^{\ast}\mathscr{Q}\right\}\in\mathcal{F}_{\text{Cat}}\end{array}\right\}

(here
\mathcal{F}_{\text{Cat}}
are the fibrations of the naive model structure on Cat), and
weak-equivalences

(17.10)

\mathcal{W}_{\text{sBio}^{\phi}}=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{l}f:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{Q},\left\{\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})\to\mathscr{Q}^{\pm}\left(f^{0}(c_{0});f^{0}(\overline{c})\right)\right\}\in\mathcal{W}_{s\text{Set}},\\
\\
\text{all $c_{0},\overline{c}\in\mathscr{P}$}\text{and}\;\pi_{0}i^{\ast}j^{\ast}\mathscr{P}\to\pi_{0}i^{\ast}j^{\ast}\mathscr{Q}\;\text{essentially surjective}\end{array}\right\}

We have the (resp trivial) generating cofibrations, so that

(17.11)

\mathcal{W}_{\text{sBio}^{\phi}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\text{sBio}^{\phi}}=\left\{\left\{\phi\to g^{\ast}I\right\}\cup\left\{g^{\ast}C_{n}^{\pm}(\partial\Delta(m))\to g^{\ast}C_{n}^{\pm}(\Delta(m))\right\}\right\}^{\perp}

(17.12)

\mathcal{F}_{\text{sBio}^{\phi}}=\left\{\left\{g^{\ast}I\rightrightarrows g^{\ast}N(0\leftrightarrow 1)\right\}\cup\left\{g^{\ast}C_{n}^{\pm}(\Lambda_{m}^{e})\to g^{\ast}C_{n}^{\pm}(\Delta(m))\right\}\right\}^{\perp}

Here
g^{\ast}:\text{sBio}\to\text{sBio}^{\phi}
is the functor that simply forgets the
constants,
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\phi)=\left\{0_{c_{0}}^{\pm}\right\}\mapsto(g^{\ast}\mathscr{P})^{\pm}(c_{0};\phi)=\phi
, so that we have the adjunctions

(17.13)

\text{sBio}^{\phi}
sBio
g_{\text{\phantom{\,}}_{!}}
g^{\ast}
g_{\ast}

(g_{{}_{!}}\mathscr{P})^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{colim}}}\limits_{\left\{{c_{1}^{\prime}\ldots c_{\ell}^{\prime}}\right\}\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{0}}\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c}c^{\prime}_{1}\cdots c_{\ell}^{\prime}),

\left(g_{\ast}\mathscr{P}\right)^{\pm}(c_{0};\overline{c})=\prod_{\phi\not=I\subseteq\left\{1\ldots n\right\}}\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(c_{0};c_{I})

The category of closed simplicial bios
sBio
gets a transfered

projective
model structure via
g^{\ast}
from
\text{sBio}^{\phi}
, so
that

(17.14)

\mathcal{F}_{\text{sBio}}\equiv\left(g^{\ast}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\text{sBio}^{\phi}}\right)\quad,\quad\mathcal{W}_{\text{sBio}}=(g^{\ast})^{-1}\left(\mathcal{W}_{\text{sBio}^{\phi}}\right)

The main lemma here is that the transfer condition are satisfied, and this follows
since
g^{\ast}g_{!}g^{\ast}C_{n}(X)=\coprod\limits_{\phi\not=I\subseteq\left\{1\ldots n\right\}}g^{\ast}C_{I}(X)
, and so the functor
g^{\ast}g_{!}
takes the generating
trivial cofibrations to trivial cofibrations.

There is an equivalent
Reedy
model structure on
sBio
, with the
same weak-equivalences, and with fibrations

(17.15)

\begin{array}[H]{lll}\widecheck{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{sBio}}&\equiv&\mathcal{F}_{\text{sBio}}\cap\left\{f:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{Q},\left\{\mathscr{P}\to(f^{0})^{\ast}\mathscr{Q}\right\}\in\widecheck{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{sBio}_{\mathscr{P}^{0}}}\right\}\\
\\
&\equiv&\left\{\left\{I\rightrightarrows N(0\leftrightarrow 1)\right\}\cup\left\{C_{n}^{\pm}(\Lambda^{e}_{m})\coprod\limits_{\widecheck{C}^{\pm}_{n}(\Lambda_{m}^{e})}\widecheck{C}_{n}^{\pm}\left(\Delta(m)\right)\to C_{n}^{\pm}\left(\Delta(m)\right)\right\}\right\}^{\perp}\end{array}

The main lemma being again that any push-out of the generating trivial
cofibrations is a weak-equivalence.

Recall that (the small object argument gives) a
normalization
\pi:\tilde{X}\rightarrow X
for any
X\in\text{bSet}
, and moreover, any
map
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
bSet
is covered by a normal map between normal objects

\tilde{f}:\tilde{X}\rightarrow\tilde{Y}
, (can take

\tilde{X}=X\prod\limits_{Y}\tilde{Y}
).

The category of bital sets
bSet
has a
model structure with the cofibrations
\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}\equiv\left\{\text{normal monos}\right\}
, the fibrant objects are
\infty\text{Bio}
, and the weak-equivalences

(17.16)

\mathcal{W}_{\text{bSet}}\equiv\left\{f:X\rightarrow Y,\tilde{f}^{\ast}:\mathscr{Q}^{\tilde{Y}}\rightarrow\mathscr{Q}^{\tilde{X}}\in\mathcal{W}_{s\text{Set}}\;\text{for all $\mathscr{Q}\in\infty\text{Bio}$}\right\}

It is cofibrantly generated, so that we have for the (resp. trivial) fibration

(17.17)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\mathcal{F}_{\text{bSet}}\equiv\left(\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}\cap\mathcal{W}_{\text{bSet}}\right)^{\perp}\equiv\Big\{\left\{I\hookrightarrow N(0\leftrightarrow 1)\right\}\cup\left\{\Lambda^{e}\mathscr{Y}_{G}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{Y}_{G}\right\}_{G\in\text{Bit},e\in\Phi^{\text{in}}(G)}\Big\}^{\perp}\\
\\
\mathcal{W}_{\text{bSet}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\text{bSet}}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}^{\perp}\equiv\left\{\partial\mathscr{Y}_{G}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{Y}_{G}\right\}^{\perp}_{G\in\text{Bit}}\end{array}

Moreover it seems that the pushout product property (for
f,g\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{bSet}}
with
f\in\mathcal{W}_{\text{bSet}}
,
f\boxtimes g\in\mathcal{W}_{g\text{Set}}^{k}
), as well as the
associativity property (inclusions
(X\otimes Y)\otimes Z\hookrightarrow X\otimes Y\otimes Z\hookleftarrow X\otimes(Y\otimes Z)
are
trivial

cofibrations) hold, and so one gets an induced associative symmetric product
\otimes
on
the homotopy category
\text{Ho}(\text{bSet})
.

A modification of the
Berger-Moerdijk-Boardman-Vogt resolution,, associating with a bit
G\in\text{Bit}
the
resolution
W\mathscr{S}_{G}
of the bio
\mathscr{S}_{G}
, gives a functor

W:\text{Bit}\to\text{sBio}
, something like

(17.18)

\left(W\mathscr{S}_{G}\right)^{\pm}\left(e_{0};e_{i}\right):=\coprod_{B\in\mathscr{S}_{G}^{\pm},\,d_{\pm}B=e_{0},\,d_{\mp}B=\left\{e_{i}\right\}}\Delta[1]^{\Phi^{\text{in}}(B)}

By Kan extension get the adjunction

(17.19)

W_{!}:\text{bSet}
\text{sBio}:W^{\ast}

and this should give a Quillen equivalence between the model structures on

bSet
and
sBio
.

18
Self-duality

Having the involution,
\text{Bio}\righttoleftarrow
,
\mathscr{P}\mapsto\mathscr{P}^{\text{op}}
, we
can speak of the self-dual bios,
\text{Bio}^{t}
, with objects the bios with involution,

(18.1)

P\mapsto P^{t}:\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{P}^{\text{op}}=(\mathscr{P}^{+},\mathscr{P}^{-}),

and with maps of bios commuting with the involution (the involution is always the identity
on
\mathscr{P}^{0}
). There is a forgetfull functor

U:\text{Bio}^{t}\to\text{Bio}
.

For
\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})\in\text{Bio}^{t}
, we
can forget
\mathscr{P}^{+}
since it is isomorphic to
\mathscr{P}^{-}
, and we only have to remember
the action
\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,
of
\mathscr{P}^{-}\equiv\mathscr{P}^{+}
on
\mathscr{P}^{-}
. Thus the
objects of
\text{Bio}^{t}
are (closed, symmetric, colored) operads
\mathscr{P}
, together
with a
contraction
operation: For a word
\overline{c}=\overline{c}_{1}\ldots\overline{c}_{n}
,
\overline{c}_{i}=(c_{i1},\cdots,c_{im_{i}})
,
b_{0},b_{1}\cdots b_{n}\in\mathscr{P}^{0}
, we have
S_{n}
-covariant and
S_{m_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{m_{n}}
- invariant functions

(18.2)

\begin{array}[H]{c}//:\mathscr{P}(b_{0};\overline{c})\times\left[\mathscr{P}(b_{1};\overline{c}_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathscr{P}(b_{n};\overline{c}_{n})\right]\longrightarrow\mathscr{P}(b_{0};b_{1}\cdots b_{n})\\
\\
(P,P_{i})\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}P//(P_{i}):=P\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(P_{i}^{t}).\end{array}

This contraction is unital

(18.3)

P//_{({\mathbf{1}}_{c_{i}})}=P

and associative

(18.4)

\left(P//(P_{ij})\right)//(P_{j})=P//\left(P_{j}\circ(P_{ij})\right)

The linearity axioms read

(18.5)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\left(P\circ(P_{i})\right)//(P_{ij})=P\circ\left(P_{i}//P_{ij}\right)\\
\\
P//\left(P_{i}//(P_{ij})\right)=\left(P\circ(P_{ij})\right)//(P_{i})\end{array}

Note that we get an involution for the underlying category
i^{\ast}\mathscr{P}\cong(i^{\ast}\mathscr{P})^{\text{op}}
, for
b,c\in\mathscr{P}^{0}
,

(18.6)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\mathscr{P}(b,c)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathscr{P}(c,b)\\
\\
P\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}P^{t}:={\mathbf{1}}_{c}//P\\
\\
(P_{1}\circ P_{2})^{t}=P_{2}^{t}\circ P_{1}^{t}\quad,\quad{\mathbf{1}}_{c}^{t}={\mathbf{1}}_{c}\quad,\quad P^{tt}=P\end{array}

Similarly we have the category of self-dual bital sets
\text{bSet}^{t}
, its objects are
bital sets
X\in\text{bSet}
together with an involution
X\cong X^{\text{op}}
, i.e. for
each
G\in\text{Bit}
we have

(18.7)

x\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}x^{t}:\;\;X_{G}\xmapsto{\;\;\sim\;\;}X_{G^{\text{op}}}\;\;\;,\;\;\;x^{tt}=x,

and natural in
G
; the maps in
\text{bSet}^{t}
are maps in
bSet
that commute
with the involution; we have a forgetfull functor
U:\text{bSet}^{t}\to\text{bSet}
.

We get an induced “self-dual” picture

s\text{Set}^{t}
\text{bSet}^{t}
i_{!}
i^{\ast}
\text{Cat}^{t}
\text{Bio}^{t}
\tau
N
i_{!}
i^{\ast}
N
\tau

with
\text{Cat}^{t}
(resp.
s\text{Set}^{t}
) the similarly defined categories of
“categories with an involution” (resp “simplicial set with an involution”).

19
Rigs

Definition 19.1
.

A
Rig
(
\equiv
Ring without Negatives) is a set
R
with two
operations of addition and multiplication

(19.2)

R\times R\rightrightarrows R\quad,\quad(x,y)\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}\begin{array}[H]{l}x+y\\
x\cdot y\end{array},

both associative and unital

(19.3)

\begin{array}[H]{lll}(x+y)+z=x+(y+z)\quad,\quad(x\cdot y)\cdot z=x\cdot(y\cdot z)\\
\\
x+0=x=0+x\quad,\quad x\cdot 1=x=1\cdot x\end{array}

with addition always commutative
x+y=y+x
, and distributive

(19.4)

\begin{array}[H]{l}(x_{1}+x_{2})\cdot y=(x_{1}\cdot y)+(x_{2}\cdot y)\quad,\quad x\cdot(y_{1}+y_{2})=(x\cdot y_{1})+(x\cdot y_{2})\\
\\
x\cdot 0=0=0\cdot x\end{array}

A rig with involution is a rig
R
with an involution

(19.5)

\begin{array}[H]{l}x\mapsto x^{t}:R\righttoleftarrow\quad,\quad\text{satisfying}\;x^{tt}=x\;\text{and}\;0^{t}=0\quad,\quad 1^{t}=1\\
\\
(x+y)^{t}=x^{t}+y^{t}\quad,\quad(x\cdot y)^{t}=y^{t}\cdot x^{t}\end{array}

A rig is commutative
R\in C\text{Rig}
if multiplication is commutative,

x\cdot y=y\cdot x

A map of rigs
\varphi:R\to R^{\prime}
is a set map preserving the operations and
constants

(19.6)

\varphi(0)=0\quad,\quad\varphi(1)=1\quad,\quad\varphi(x+y)=\varphi(x)+\varphi(y)\quad,\quad\varphi(x\cdot y)=\varphi(x)\cdot\varphi(y)

and in the self-dual case
\text{Rig}^{t}
,
\varphi
should also preserve the involution,

\varphi(x^{t})=\varphi(x)^{t}.

Thus we have categories and functors

\displaystyle\begin{array}[H]{l}\hbox to121.83pt{\vbox to62.84pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-15.78987pt\lower 34.95724pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

with the usual multiplication
x\cdot y
, and with the “tropical” addition

(19.9)

x+y:\overset{def}{=}\max\left\{x,y\right\}.

For
A\in\text{Rig}
we have the
monochromatic
operad
\mathscr{P}_{A}
(i.e.

\mathscr{P}^{0}=\left\{\ast\right\}
, and we write
\mathscr{P}_{A}^{-}(n)\equiv\mathscr{P}_{A}(\ast;\underbrace{\ast\cdots\ast}_{n})
)
with
\mathscr{P}_{A}^{-}(n)\equiv A^{n}
, and with compositions

(19.10)

o_{i}:A^{n}\times A^{m}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}A^{n+m-1}

(a_{1},\cdots,a_{i},\cdots a_{n})\circ_{i}(b_{1},\cdots,b_{m}):=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{i-1},a_{i}\cdot b_{1},a_{i}\cdot b_{2}\cdots a_{i}\cdot b_{m},a_{i+1}\cdots a_{m})

We have similarly the operad
\mathscr{P}_{A}^{+}\equiv\mathscr{P}_{A^{\text{op}}}
of colume vectors

(19.11)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\mathscr{P}_{A}^{+}(\underbrace{\ast\cdots\ast}_{n};\ast)\equiv\mathscr{P}_{A}^{+}(n)\equiv A^{n}\quad,\quad\text{with composition}\\
\\
A^{n}\times A^{m}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}A^{m+n-1}\;\;\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\\
\vdots\\
a_{n}\end{pmatrix}\circ_{j}\begin{pmatrix}b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
b_{j}\\
\vdots\\
b_{m}\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
a_{1}\cdot b_{j}\\
\vdots\\
a_{n}\cdot b_{j}\\
\vdots\\
b_{m}\end{pmatrix}\end{array}

Moreover, we have mutual actions, for
1\leq k\leq m\leq n
,

(19.12)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\overleftarrow{\circ}_{k}:A^{n}\times A^{m-k+1}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}A^{n-m+k}\\
\\
\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\cdots a_{k}\cdots a_{m}\cdots a_{n}\end{pmatrix}\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,_{k}\begin{pmatrix}b_{0}\\
b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
b_{m-k}\end{pmatrix}:=\\
\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\cdots a_{k-1},a_{k}\cdot b_{0}+\cdots+a_{k+j}\cdot b_{j}+\cdots+a_{m}\cdot b_{m-k},a_{m+1}\cdots a_{n}\end{pmatrix}\end{array}

and similarly for
\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,
.

This construction gives a full and faithful embedding

(19.13)

\text{Rig}\;\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

Moreover, when the rig
A
has an involution, the bio
\mathscr{P}_{A}=\left(\mathscr{P}^{-}_{A},\mathscr{P}^{+}_{A}\right)
has an involution

(19.14)

(a_{1},\cdots,a_{n})^{t}:=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}^{t}\\
\vdots\\
a_{n}^{t}\end{pmatrix}

giving

(19.15)

\text{Rig}^{t}\hbox to57.31pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

The initial object of
Rig
, and of
\text{Rig}^{t}
, is
\mathbb{N}
the rig of natural
numbers, therefore, the above functor gives
Bios
under
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{N}}
. Having the
vectors
(1,1)\in\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{N}}^{\pm}(2)\to\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(2)
we can define addition on

\mathscr{P}(1)
making it into a rig

(19.16)

a+b:=(1,1)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(a,b)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\begin{pmatrix}1\\
1\end{pmatrix}.

We get adjunctions,

(19.17)

\mathscr{P}_{A}
\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{N}}^{\text{\small$\backslash\text{Bio}^{t}_{\left\{\ast\right\}}$}}
\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{N}}^{\text{ \small$\backslash\text{Bio}_{\{\ast\}}$}}
\mathscr{P}
A
\text{Rig}^{t}
Rig
\mathscr{P}(1)
U
U

20
The
\ell_{p}
bio

Fix
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, with
1/p+1/q=1
. We have the monochromatic operad

\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{p}}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
, with

(20.1)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{p}}(n):=\left\{(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}(n)\equiv\mathbb{R}^{n},x_{1}^{p}+\cdots+x_{n}^{p}\leq 1\right\}\\
\\
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\circ_{i}(y_{1},\cdots,y_{m}):=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{i-1},x_{i}\cdot y_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\cdot y_{m},x_{i+1},\cdots,x_{n})\end{array}

We write the elements of
\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{q}}
as column vector, and we have the mutual actions
induced from
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
,

(20.2)

(a_{1}\cdots a_{k}\cdots a_{n})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,_{k}\begin{pmatrix}b_{0}\\
b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
b_{m-k}\end{pmatrix}:=(a_{1}\cdots a_{k-1},a_{k}\cdot b_{0}+\cdots+a_{k+j}\cdot b_{j}+\cdots+a_{m}\cdot b_{m-k},a_{m+1},\cdots,a_{n})

and similarly for
\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,
.

The rig
\mathbb{R}
, being commutative has an (identity) involution, hence
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
has an
involution

(20.3)

(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})^{t}:=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\\
\vdots\\
a_{n}\end{pmatrix}

The sub-bio
\left(\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{p}},\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{q}}\right)\subseteq\left(\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{-},\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\right)
is stable under this involution iff
p=q=2
, and we are in the self-dual case

(20.4)

\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{2}}(n)\equiv\left\{(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n},x_{1}^{2}+\cdots+x_{n}^{2}\leq 1\right\}

the
\ell_{2}
-unit ball.

The bio
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}:=(\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{2}},\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{2}})\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
is the “Real integers”,
analogue at the “Real prime” of the
p
-adic integers
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
p
a prime. We similarly have the “complex integers”
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{C}}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
given by the unit
\ell_{2}
complex balls.

21
Distributive bios

Let
\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})
be a monochromatic bio,
\mathscr{P}^{0}=\left\{\ast\right\}
.

We say
\mathscr{P}
is
distributive
if the actions and compositions

interchange
:

for
P\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)
,
P^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(m)
,

P^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(\ell)
,

(21.1)

\left(P\overleftarrow{\circ}_{\hskip-2.84526ptj}\,P^{\prime}\right){\circ}_{j}P^{\prime\prime}\equiv(P\circ\underbrace{(P^{\prime\prime},\cdots,P^{\prime\prime})}_{m})\sigma_{m,\ell}\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\underbrace{\left(P^{\prime},\cdots,P^{\prime}\right)}_{\ell}

and similarly,

(21.2)

P^{\prime\prime}\circ_{j}\left(P^{\prime}\overrightarrow{\circ}_{\hskip-2.84526ptj}\,P\right)\equiv(\underbrace{P^{\prime},\cdots,P^{\prime}}_{\ell})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,\sigma_{\ell,m}((\underbrace{P^{\prime\prime},\cdots,P^{\prime\prime}}_{m})\circ P)

for
P\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)
,
P^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(m)
,
P^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(\ell)
.

Pictorially,

(21.3)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\hbox to299.56pt{\vbox to63.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower 25.20749pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

We let
\text{CBio}\subseteq\text{Bio}
denote the full subcategory of
distributive bios.

For a planer bit
G\in p\text{Bit}
, and
e\in G^{1}
with

d_{-}e=v_{-}\in C_{n1}(G)
,
d_{+}e=v_{+}\in C_{1m}(G)
, we define
GZe
, the

interchange of
G
at
e
, by applying to
G
the
perculation move
:
moving
C_{v_{+}}
to the left of
C_{v_{-}}
, pictorially

(21.4)
