Using these percolation moves we can bring all the
C_{v},v\in C^{-}(G)
, to the left of
all
C_{u},u\in C^{+}(G)
. Thus for
G\in p\text{Bit}_{n,m}(G)
, we obtain after a finite
number of such perculations

(21.5)

G\Rrightarrow GZe_{1}\Rrightarrow GZe_{1}Ze_{2}\Rrightarrow\cdots GZe_{1}\cdots Ze_{\ell}\equiv(T_{i},\sigma,T_{j}^{\prime})

with
\left\{T_{i}\right\}_{i=1\ldots n}
,
\left\{T_{j}^{\prime}\right\}_{j=1\ldots m}
are forest of planer trees, and
\sigma
is a bijection between their leaves

\sigma:\coprod\limits_{1\leq i\leq n}\partial T_{i}\xrightarrow[\hskip 16.38895pt]{\sim}\coprod\limits_{1\leq j\leq m}\partial T_{j}^{\prime}
.

For a distributive bio
\mathscr{P}
,
G\in\text{Bit}
,
e\in G^{1}
with

v_{-}=d_{-}e\in C^{+}(G)
,
v_{+}=d_{+}e\in C^{-}(G)
, we clearly have

(21.6)

\begin{array}[H]{lll}(N\mathscr{P})_{G}&\equiv&\text{Bio}(\mathscr{S}_{G},\mathscr{P})\\
\\
&\equiv&\left\{\begin{array}[H]{ll}\varphi\in\text{Bio}(\mathscr{S}_{Gze},\mathscr{P}),\\
\varphi^{\circ}(e_{i,j})=\varphi^{\circ}(e_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}),\\
\varphi(v_{-}^{(i)})=\varphi(v_{-}^{(i^{\prime})}),\\
\varphi(v_{+}^{(j)})=\varphi(v_{+}^{(j^{\prime})})\end{array}\right\}\\
\\
&\subseteq&(N\mathscr{P})_{GZe}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq(N\mathscr{P})_{(T_{i},\sigma,T_{j}^{\prime})}\end{array}

For a distributive bio
\mathscr{P}
, the underliying (associative, unital)
monoid
\mathscr{P}(\ast;\ast)=\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(1)
is
commutative
. Indeed, for unary
operations (
n=m=p=1
) interchange reduces to commutativity

(21.7)

(P\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,P^{\prime})\circ P^{\prime\prime}\equiv(P\circ P^{\prime\prime})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,P^{\prime}

and for
P=1_{\ast}
,
P^{\prime}=Q\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,1_{\ast}=\overrightarrow{Q}
, this gives

(21.8)

\begin{array}[H]{lll}Q\circ P^{\prime\prime}=\overset{\leftrightarrows}{Q}\circ P^{\prime\prime}&\equiv(1_{\ast}\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\overrightarrow{Q})\circ P^{\prime\prime}\equiv(1_{\ast}\circ P^{\prime\prime})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(Q\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,1_{\ast})\\
\\
&\equiv\left(P^{\prime\prime}\circ Q\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,1_{\ast}\equiv P^{\prime\prime}\circ Q\end{array}

Moreover, interchange imply this monoid
\mathscr{P}(1)
acts
centrally
on operations
and co-operations, in the sense that for
a\in\mathscr{P}(1)
,
P\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)

(resp.
P\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)
) we have

(21.9)

a\circ P=P\circ(\underbrace{a,\cdots,a}_{n})\quad,\quad(\text{resp.
$P\circ a=(\underbrace{a,\cdots,a}_{n})\circ P$})

For a
multiplicative subset

S\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)

(21.10)

s_{1},s_{2}\in S\Longrightarrow s_{1}\circ s_{2}\in S,\;\;{\mathbf{1}}\in S,

we have the
localization
of
\mathscr{P}
at
S
,
S^{-1}\mathscr{P}
:

(21.11)

\begin{array}[H]{l}S^{-1}\mathscr{P}\equiv\mathscr{P}[S^{-1}]\equiv(S^{-1}\mathscr{P}^{-},S^{-1}\mathscr{P}^{+})\\
\\
S^{-1}\mathscr{P}^{\pm}=\mathscr{P}^{\pm}\times S/\sim\end{array}

with
(P^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\sim(P^{\prime},s^{\prime})
iff
s\circ s^{\prime}\circ P^{\prime\prime}=s\circ s^{\prime\prime}\circ P^{\prime}
for some
s\in S
; one writes

P/s=\frac{1}{s}\circ P
for the equivalence class
(P,s)/\sim
.

For a commutative rig

A
the bio
\mathscr{P}_{A}
is distributive; this is true in particular for commutative
rings
A
, and we shall continue and define ideals, primes, spectrum, schemes etc. for
distributive bios making sure that we get the right definitions for

\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}_{A}
,
A\in C\text{Ring}
.

Remark 21.12
.

For any rig
A
, the associated bio
\mathscr{P}_{A}
contains the (distributive,
self-dual) sub-bio
\mathbb{F}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{A}
with

(21.13)

\begin{array}[H]{ll}\mathbb{F}^{-}(n)&=\left\{0=(0,\cdots,0),\delta_{1}=(1,0,\cdots,0),\cdots,\delta_{n}=(0,\cdots 0,1)\right\}\\
\\
\mathbb{F}^{+}(n)&=\left\{0^{t}=\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{matrix}\right),\delta_{1}^{t}=\left(\begin{matrix}1\\
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{matrix}\right),\cdots,\delta_{n}^{t}=\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
\vdots\\
0\\
1\end{matrix}\right)\right\}\end{array}

the (co)-vectors with at most one
1
coordinate (note that
\mathbb{F}
is closed
under the composition and action operations); we call
\mathbb{F}

“
the field with one element
”. Although we can develop everything
using arbitrary distributive bios, we will
restrict our attention to “
\mathbb{F}
-algegras”, and always assume our
distributive bio
\mathscr{P}
contains the bio
\mathbb{F}\subseteq\mathscr{P}
(note that

\mathbb{F}
is indeed a “field”: if
\varphi\in\text{Bio}(\mathbb{F},\mathscr{P})

is not an injection then
\varphi(0)=\varphi(1)\in\mathscr{P}(1)
, and
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(n)\equiv\left\{\ast\right\}
reduce to a point,
\mathscr{P}
is the zero-bio, the final object of
bio). We will use
\mathbb{F}\subseteq\mathscr{P}
beginning in section
29
, to have
the sphere spectrum and “homological algebra”. This is just for convenience (one can replace
everywhere “
\phi_{\ast}(V_{+})
” by the “free
\mathscr{P}\text{-set}
on
V
”). Thus our basic building blocks for
geometry will be the (non-zero) objects of the full subcategory

(21.14)

C\text{Bio}\subseteq\mathbb{F}^{\displaystyle\backslash\text{Bio}}

of distributive bios under
\mathbb{F}
.

22
The spectrum

Fix
\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})\in\text{CBio}
.

Definition 22.1
.

A
1
-ideal
is a subset
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)
such that for

a_{1}\cdots a_{n}\in\mathfrak{a}
,
b\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)
,
d\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)
, the

“linear combination”

(22.2)

\left\{b,(a_{i}),d\right\}\overset{\text{def}}{=}\left(b\circ(a_{i})\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d=b\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,\left((a_{i})\circ d\right)\in\mathfrak{a}\;\;\;\text{is in $\mathfrak{a}$}

Note that intersection of ideals is again an ideal, so we can speak of the
1
-ideal
\mathfrak{a}

generated by a set
\left\{a_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1);\mathfrak{a}
is also given as
the collection of all linear - combinations of the
a_{i}
’s (with repetition).

A proper
1
-ideal
\mathfrak{p}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)
, with
1\not\in\mathfrak{p}
, is called
prime
if

S_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\mathfrak{p}
is multiplicative. The set of primes is denoted

\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
.

The set
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
is not empty: a maximal proper ideal
1\not\in\mathfrak{p}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)
, which exists by Zorn’s lemma, is always prime. Indeed, if

a,a^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\mathfrak{p}
, then for some linear combinations

\left\{b,(a_{i})_{i\leq n},d\right\}={\mathbf{1}}=\left\{b^{\prime},(a^{\prime}_{j})_{j\leq m},d^{\prime}\right\},b,b^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{-},d,d^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{+},

a_{i}\in\mathfrak{p}
or
a_{i}=a
,
a_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{p}
or

a_{j}^{\prime}=a^{\prime}
and by distributivity we have

\begin{array}[H]{lll}1=1\circ 1&=&\left(\left(b\circ(a_{i})\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d\right)\circ\left(\left(b^{\prime}\circ(a_{j}^{\prime})\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d^{\prime}\right)\\
\\
&=&\left(b\circ(\underbrace{b^{\prime},\cdots b^{\prime}}_{n})\right)\sigma\circ(a_{i}\circ a_{j}^{\prime})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\sigma^{\prime}\left((\underbrace{d,\cdots,d}_{m})\circ d^{\prime}\right)\end{array}

with
a_{i}\circ a_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{p}
or
a_{i}\circ a_{j}^{\prime}=a\circ a^{\prime}
,
and so
a\circ a^{\prime}\not\in\mathfrak{p}
.

Moreover,
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
is a (compact,
sober
\equiv
Zariski) topological space with respect to the
Zariski topology
with
closed sets

(22.3)

\begin{array}[H]{l}V(\mathfrak{a}):=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}),\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{a}\right\}\quad,\quad\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)\;\text{ideal},\\
\\
V(\mathfrak{a})\cap V(\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})=V(\mathfrak{a}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})\quad,\quad\mathfrak{a}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=\text{the ideal generated by $\left\{a\cdot a^{\prime},a\in\mathfrak{a},a^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}\right\}$};\\
\\
\bigcup\limits_{i}V(\mathfrak{a}_{i})=V(\sum\limits_{i}\mathfrak{a}_{i})\quad,\quad\sum\limits_{i}\mathfrak{a}_{i}\;\;\text{the ideal generated by
$\cup_{i}\mathfrak{a}_{i}$};\\
\\
V(0)=\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\quad,\quad V(1)=\phi\end{array}

We have a basis for the topology by
basic open sets

(22.4)

\begin{array}[H]{c}D(f):=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}),\mathfrak{p}\not\ni f\right\}\quad,\quad f\in\mathscr{P}(1).\\
\\
D(f_{1})\cap D(f_{2})=D\left(f_{1}\circ f_{2}\right)\quad,\quad D(1)=\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\quad,\quad D(0)=\phi,\\
\\
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\setminus V(\mathfrak{a})=\bigcup_{f\in\mathfrak{a}}D(f)\end{array}

For a map of distributive bios
\varphi\in\text{CBio}(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q})
, the pull back of a
(prime) ideal is a (prime) ideal, and we obtain

(22.5)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\text{spec}(\varphi)=\varphi^{\ast}:\text{spec}(\mathscr{Q})\longrightarrow\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\\
\\
\varphi^{\ast}(\mathfrak{q})=\varphi^{-1}(\mathfrak{q})=\left\{a\in\mathscr{P}(1),\varphi(a)\in\mathfrak{q}\right\}.\end{array}

It is a continuous map:

(22.6)

\varphi^{\ast^{-1}}\big(V(\mathfrak{a})\big)=\left\{\mathfrak{q}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{Q}),\varphi^{-1}(\mathfrak{q})\supseteq\mathfrak{a}\right\}=V\big(\varphi(\mathfrak{a})\big)

and similarly the pull back of basic open set is again basic open

(22.7)

\varphi^{\ast^{-1}}\left(D(f)\right)=\left\{\mathfrak{q}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{Q}),\varphi^{-1}(\mathfrak{q})\not\ni f\right\}=D\big(\varphi(f)\big).

Thus we have a functor

(22.8)

\text{spec}:(\text{CBio})^{\text{op}}\longrightarrow\text{Top}\quad\text{(compact, sober)}

We have a Galois correspondence (
\equiv
adjunction of order sets)

(22.9)

\left\{\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)\;\text{ideal}\right\}
V
I
\left\{Z\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\;\text{closed}\right\}

\bigcap\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in Z}\mathfrak{p}=:I(Z)\;\hbox to57.31pt{\vbox to5.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.55 0 L 78.33 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{56.70552pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.18 L 0 -3.18}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;Z

We have
VI(Z)=Z
, and

(22.10)

IV(\mathfrak{a})=\bigcap\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{p}=\left\{a\in\mathscr{P}(1),a^{n}=\underbrace{a\circ\ldots\circ a}_{n}\in\mathfrak{a}\;\text{for $n>>0$}\right\}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}}

We get induced bijections

(22.11)

\sim
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
\left\{Z\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\;\text{closed irreducible}\right\}

\subseteq

\subseteq

\left\{Z\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\;\text{closed }\right\}
\left\{\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1),\mathfrak{a}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}}\right\}
V
\sim
I

For a multiplicative set
S\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)
, let
\phi_{S}:\mathscr{P}\rightarrow S^{-1}\mathscr{P}
,
\phi_{S}(P)=P/1
, be the canonical map, it induces a homeomorphism

(22.12)

\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}),\mathfrak{p}\cap S=\phi\right\}
\phi_{S}^{\ast}
S^{-1}
\sim
\text{spec}(S^{-1}\mathscr{P})

In particular, for
S_{f}=\left\{f^{\mathbb{N}}\right\}=\left\{f^{n},n\geq 0\right\}
,

f\in\mathscr{P}(1)
, we get

(22.13)

\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\supseteq D(f)\xleftrightarrow[\hskip 16.38895pt]{\sim}\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}_{f}),\qquad\mathscr{P}_{f}:=\left\{f^{\mathbb{N}}\right\}^{-1}\mathscr{P}

and for
S_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\mathfrak{p}
,
\mathfrak{p}
prime we get

(22.14)

\left\{q\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}),q\subseteq\mathfrak{p}\right\}\xleftrightarrow[\hskip 16.38895pt]{\sim}\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}),\;\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}:=S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\mathscr{P}

i.e.
\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}
is a
local
bio in the sense that

(22.15)

m_{\mathfrak{p}}:=\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}(1)\setminus\left\{x\in\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}(1),\exists x^{-1}\;\text{with}\;x\circ x^{-1}=1=x^{-1}\circ x\right\}=S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})

is the unique maximal ideal of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

We make
\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}
into a category by

(22.16)

\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q})=\left\{\varphi\in\text{CBio}(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q}),\varphi\;\text{is local:
$\varphi^{\ast}(m_{\mathscr{Q}})=m_{\mathscr{P}}$}\right\}

23
The structure sheaf
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}

Fix
\mathscr{P}\in\text{CBio}
. For an open set
\mathscr{U}\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
define

(23.1)

\mathscr{O}^{\pm}_{\mathscr{P}}(n):=\left\{f:\mathscr{U}\to\coprod\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathscr{U}}\mathscr{P}^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{p}}(n),f(\mathfrak{p})\in\mathscr{P}^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{p}}(n),\;\text{and $f$ is
{\it{\bf{locally a fraction}}}}\right\}

f

locally a fraction
: for all
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathscr{U}
, there exists open

\mathfrak{p}\in U_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq U
, and
P\in\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(n)
,
s\in\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\bigcup\limits_{\mathfrak{q}\in U_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathfrak{q}
, such that for all

\mathfrak{q}\in\mathscr{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
we have
f(\mathfrak{q})\equiv P/s
in
\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Note that
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathscr{U})=\left(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}^{-}(\mathscr{U}),\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}^{+}(\mathscr{U})\right)
is a distributive bio, the
operations of compositions and actions are defined pointwise (in each
\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}
), and the
“local fraction condition” is preserved. For open sets
\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathscr{U}\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
we have the restriction maps

(23.2)

\rho_{\mathscr{V}}^{\mathscr{U}}:\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U})\to\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{V})

making
\mathscr{U}\mapsto\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U})
a pre-sheaf of bios over
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
; by the local
nature of the “locally-fraction-condition” it is clearly a sheaf.

For
\mathfrak{p}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
, the
stalk
of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}
at

\mathfrak{p}
is given by

(23.3)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P},\mathfrak{p}}:=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{\mathscr{U}\ni\mathfrak{p}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathscr{U})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}\\
\\
(\mathscr{U},f)_{/\approx}\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}f(\mathfrak{p})\end{array}

it is well defined, surjective, and injective.

The global sections of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}
are given by

Theorem 3
.

For a basic open set
D(s)\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
,
s\in\mathscr{P}(1)
,

\begin{array}[H]{c}\Psi:\mathscr{P}_{s}=\mathscr{P}\left[\frac{1}{s}\right]\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}\left(D(s)\right)\\
\\
\Psi\left(P/s^{n}\right):=\left\{f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P/s^{n}\;\text{in
$\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}$ for all $\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)$}\right\}.\end{array}

For
s=1
we obtain the global sections

\mathscr{P}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}))

Proof.

The map
\Psi
which takes
P/s^{n}\in\mathscr{P}_{s}
to the constant section

f
,
f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P/s^{n}
, is a well defined map of bios. Take
P\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(k)
, the case
P\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(k)
is similar.

\Psi
is injective
: Assume
\Psi\left(P_{1}/s^{n_{1}}\right)=\Psi(P_{2}/s^{n_{2}})
, and define

\mathfrak{a}:=\text{An}\left(s^{n_{2}}\circ P_{1};s^{n_{1}}\circ P_{2}\right)=\left\{a\in\mathscr{P}(1),a\circ s^{n_{2}}\circ P_{1}=a\circ s^{n_{1}}\circ P_{2}\right\};

by distributivity it follows
\mathfrak{a}
is a
1
-ideal. We have

P_{1}/s^{n_{1}}=P_{2}/s^{n_{2}}\;\;\text{in}\;\;\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\;\text{for all}\;\;\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)

\Rightarrow

s_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ s^{n_{2}}\circ P_{1}=s_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ s^{n_{1}}\circ P_{2}
for some
s_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\mathfrak{p}
,
all
\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)
.

\Rightarrow

\mathfrak{a}\not\subseteq\mathfrak{p}
, all

\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)

\Rightarrow

V(\mathfrak{a})\cap D(s)=\emptyset\Rightarrow V(\mathfrak{a})\subseteq V(s)\Rightarrow s\in IV(\mathfrak{a})=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}}

\Rightarrow

s^{n}\in\mathfrak{a}
for
n>>0\Rightarrow s^{n+n_{2}}\circ P_{1}=s^{n+n_{1}}\circ P_{2}
for
n>>0

\Rightarrow

P_{1}/s^{n_{1}}=P_{2}/s^{n_{2}}
in
\mathscr{P}_{s}
.

\Psi
is surjective
: Fix
f\in\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(D(s))^{-}(k)
. Since
D(s)
is compact we can cover it by
finite collection of basic open sets

D(s)=D(s_{1})\cup\cdots\cup D(s_{N})

with

f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P_{i}/t_{i}\;\text{for}\;\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i})\quad;\quad t_{i}\in\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\bigcup\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i})}\mathfrak{p}.

We have
V(t_{i})\subseteq V(s_{i})
, so
s_{i}^{n_{i}}=c_{i}\circ t_{i}
for some

c_{i}\in\mathscr{P}(1)
, and for
\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i})
,
f(\mathfrak{p})=P_{i}/t_{i}=c_{i}\circ P_{i}/s_{i}^{n_{i}}
. We can replace
s_{i}
by
s_{i}^{n_{i}}
,
(
D(s_{i})=D(s_{i}^{n_{i}})
), and
P_{i}
by
c_{i}\circ P_{i}
, so

f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P_{i}/s_{i}\quad\text{for}\;\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i}).

On the set
D(s_{i})\cap D(s_{j})=D(s_{i}\circ s_{j})
, the section
f
is given
by both
P_{i}/s_{i}
and
P_{j}/s_{j}
, and by the injectivity of
\Psi

(s_{i}\circ s_{j})^{n}\circ s_{j}\circ P_{i}=(s_{i}\circ s_{j})^{n}\circ s_{i}\circ P_{j}

By finiteness we may assume one
n
works for all
i,j
.

Replacing
s_{i}
by

s_{i}^{n+1}
, and replacing
s_{i}^{n}\circ P_{i}
by
P_{i}
, we may assume

\begin{array}[H]{l}f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P_{i}/s_{i}\quad\text{for all
$\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i})$}\\
\\
s_{j}\circ P_{i}=s_{i}\circ P_{j}\quad\text{all $i,j$.}\end{array}

Since
D(s)\subseteq\bigcup\limits_{i}D(s_{i})
we have that some power

s^{M}
is a linear-combination of the
s_{i}

\begin{array}[H]{l}s^{M}=\left\{b,(c_{j}),d\right\}=b\circ(c_{j})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d\quad,\quad c_{j}=s_{i(j)}\quad,\quad b,d\in\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(\ell)\quad,\\
\\
j\in\left\{1,\cdots,\ell\right\}\xrightarrow{i}\left\{1,\cdots,N\right\}.\end{array}

Define
P=b\circ(P_{i(j)})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(\underbrace{d,\ldots,d}_{k})
.

We have,

\begin{array}[H]{lll}s_{j}\circ P&=&b\circ\left(s_{j}\circ P_{i(j)}\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(d,\cdots,d)\equiv b\circ\left(s_{i(j)}\circ P_{j}\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(d,\cdots,d)\\
\\
&=&(b\circ(c_{j}\circ P_{j}))\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(d,\cdots,d)\\
\\
&=&((b\circ(c_{j}))\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d)\circ P_{j}\qquad\text{by distributivity}\\
\\
&=&s^{M}\circ P_{j}\end{array}

Thus
f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P_{j}/s_{j}\equiv P/s^{M}
is constant.
∎

24
Generalized Schemes

For a topological space
\mathcal{X}
, we have the category
\text{CBio}/_{\mathcal{X}}
of
sheaves of
CBio
over
\mathcal{X}
, its maps are natural transformations

\varphi=\left\{\varphi_{\mathscr{U}}\right\}
,
\varphi_{\mathscr{U}}\in\text{CBio}(\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U}),\mathscr{P}^{\prime}(\mathscr{U}))
. Putting all these categories together we have
the category
\text{CBio}/\text{Top}
: its object are pairs
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P})
,

\mathcal{X}\in\text{Top}
,
\mathscr{P}\in\text{CBio}/\mathcal{X}
, and its maps

f:(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P})\to(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})
are pairs
f\in\text{Top}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
and
f^{\natural}\in\text{CBio}/_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}(\mathscr{P}^{\prime},f_{\ast}\mathscr{P})
; explicitly,
f
is a
continuous function, and for
\mathscr{U}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
open, we have the map of bios

f_{\mathscr{U}}^{\natural}\in\text{CBio}\left(\mathscr{P}^{\prime}(\mathscr{U}),\mathscr{P}\left(f^{-1}(\mathscr{U})\right)\right)
,
these maps being compatible with restrictions.

Remark 24.1
.

For
f\in\text{Top}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
we have adjunction

\text{CBio}/\mathcal{X}
\text{CBio}/\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
f^{\ast}
f_{\ast}
f^{\ast}\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=
sheaf associated
f_{\ast}\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U})=\mathscr{P}(f^{-1}\mathscr{U})

to the pre-sheaf

\hskip-170.71652pt\mathcal{X}\supseteq\mathscr{V}\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}\hskip-28.45274pt\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{\begin{array}[H]{l}\hskip 28.45274pt\mathscr{U}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\,\text{open}\\
\hskip 28.45274ptf(\mathscr{V})\subseteq\mathscr{U}\end{array}}\hskip-19.91692pt\mathscr{P}^{\prime}(\mathscr{U})

For a map
f\in\text{CBio}/{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)
, and for a point
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we get an induced map of stalks

(24.2)

f_{x}^{\natural}:\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{f(x)}=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{f(x)\in\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathscr{P}^{\prime}(\mathscr{V})\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{f(x)\in\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathscr{P}(f^{-1}\mathscr{V})\to\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{x\in\mathscr{U}\subseteq\mathcal{X}}\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U})=\mathscr{P}_{x}

Definition 24.3
.

The category of
locally-bio-spaces

\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}
, is the category with object

(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P})\in\text{CBio}/_{\text{Top}}
, such that for all
x\in\mathcal{X}

the stalk
\mathscr{P}_{x}
is a local bio with the unique maximal proper
1
-ideal

m_{x}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{x}(1)
; the maps

f\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)
are maps
f\in\text{CBio}/_{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)
such that for all
x\in X
,
f^{\natural}_{x}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}\left(\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{f(x)},\mathscr{P}_{x}\right)
is a local map:

f_{x}^{\natural}(m_{f(x)})\subseteq m_{x}
.

Theorem 4
.

We have the adjunction

\left(\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}\right)^{\text{op}}
CBio
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}):=\left(\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}),\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}\right)
spec
\Gamma

\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}):=\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})
the global sections

\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)\equiv\text{CBio}\left(\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)

Proof.

For a point
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we have canonical map
\phi_{x}\in\text{CBio}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X}),\mathscr{P}_{x}\right)
,
\phi_{x}P=P|_{x}
the stalk of the global
section
P
at the point
x
, and we get a prime

\mathfrak{p}_{x}:=\phi_{x}^{-1}(m_{x})\in\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
,
m_{x}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{x}(1)
the maximal ideal. For a basic open set
D(s)\subseteq\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
,
s\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})(1)
, we have
\left\{x\in X,\mathfrak{p}_{x}\in D(s)\right\}=\left\{x\in X,\phi_{x}(s)\not\in m_{x}\right\}
is
open
in

\mathcal{X}
: if
\phi_{x}(s)\not\in m_{x}
, it is invertible in
\mathscr{P}_{x}
,

a_{x}\circ\phi_{x}(s)=1
,
a_{x}\in\mathscr{P}_{x}
and there is an open

\mathscr{U}_{x}\ni x
,

a\in\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U}_{x})
with
a_{x}=a|_{x}
; taking
\mathscr{U}_{x}
smaller we have
a\circ s|_{\mathscr{U}_{x}}=1
already in
\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U}_{x})
, and so
\phi_{x^{\prime}}(s)\not\in m_{x^{\prime}}
for all
x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{U}_{x}
. Thus the map

x\mapsto\mathfrak{p}_{x}
is a continuous map

\mathfrak{p}:\mathcal{X}\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}\to\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
.
The uniqueness of the inverse
\left(s|_{u_{x}}\right)^{-1}
, shows these
local inverses glue to a global inverse
s^{-1}\in\mathscr{P}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\left(D(s)\right)\right)
and we get the map of
CBio

(24.4)

\mathfrak{p}_{D(s)}^{\natural}:\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})_{s}\equiv\left\{s^{\mathbb{N}}\right\}^{-1}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathscr{P}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\left(D(s)\right)\right)

These maps are compatible on intersections,
D(s_{1})\cap D(s_{2})=D(s_{1}\circ s_{2})
, so by the sheaf property give
\mathfrak{p}_{u}^{\natural}\in\text{CBio}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\text{spec}\;\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})}(\mathscr{U}),\mathscr{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\mathscr{U})\right)
for any open
\mathscr{U}\subseteq\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
, compatible with
restrictions, so

(24.5)

\mathfrak{p}=(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\natural})\in\text{CBio}/_{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)\right)

For
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we get

(24.6)

\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{\natural}=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{\phi_{x}(s)\not\in m_{x}}\mathfrak{p}^{\natural}_{D(s)}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})_{\mathfrak{p}_{x}},\mathscr{P}_{x}\right)

is
local
, so
\mathfrak{p}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}/\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)\right)

is the co-unit of adjunction.

Given
\varphi\in\text{CBio}\left(A,\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
we get
\text{spec}(\varphi)\circ\mathfrak{p}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}/\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}(A)\right)
.

Given
f\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}/\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}(A)\right)
we get the global sections

(24.7)

\Gamma(f)=f_{\text{spec}(A)}^{\natural}\in\text{CBio}(A,\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})).

One checks these are inverse bijections.
∎

Following the footsteps of Grothendieck
we can define the category of
Generalized Schemes

\text{GSch}\subseteq\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}
to be the full subcategory of

\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}
consisting of the object

(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
which are locally affine: we have some open
cover
\mathcal{X}=\bigcup\mathscr{U}_{i}
, and
(\mathscr{U}_{i},\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathscr{U}_{i}})\cong\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathscr{U}_{i})\right)
.

An open subset of a scheme is again a scheme.

Schemes can be glued along open subsets and consistent glueing data.

Since ordinary commutative rings
A
give distributive bios
\mathscr{P}_{A}\in\text{CBio}
and since all our definitions reduce to their classical analogues for

\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}_{A}
,
A\in C\text{Ring}
, ordinary schemes embeds fully faithfully in generalized
schemes,

\text{Sch}\hookrightarrow\text{GSch},(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\mapsto(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}}})

Theorem 5
.

The category
GSch
has fiber products, for
f_{i}\in\text{GSch}(X_{i},Y)
:

X_{0}\prod\limits_{Y}X_{1}
X_{0}
X_{1}
Y
f_{0}
f_{1}

Proof.

Exactly as for ordinary schemes. Write

Y=\bigcup\limits_{i}\text{spec}(B_{i})
,

X_{\varepsilon}=\bigcup\limits_{i,j}\text{spec}(A_{j,i}^{\varepsilon})
,
\varepsilon=0,1

with
f_{\varepsilon}\left(\text{spec}(A_{j,i}^{\varepsilon})\right)\subseteq\text{spec}\;(B_{i})
, and glue:

(24.8)

X_{0}\prod\limits_{Y}X_{1}\equiv\coprod_{i,j_{0},j_{1}}\text{spec}\left(A^{0}_{j_{0},i}\coprod\limits_{B_{i}}A_{j_{1},i}^{1}\right).

Note that one uses the push-out

A^{0}_{j_{0},i}\coprod\limits_{B_{i}}A_{j_{1},i}^{1}
in
CBio

(Not the tensor product!).
∎

Remark 24.9
.

For (the initial object)
\mathbb{Z}\in C\text{Ring}\subseteq C\text{Rig}
, the
“arithmetical surface”

\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\equiv\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\prod\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}\right)

does not reduce to its diagonal, as it does in classical algebraic geometry where

\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})\prod\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})=\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z}\otimes\mathbb{Z})=\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})=\left\{(0);(2),(3),(5),(7)\cdots\right\}.

25
A
-Sets

We will denote our new “generalized rings”, the distributive bios under

\mathbb{F}
,
CBio
, by the
letters
A,B,\cdots,

Definition 25.1
.

For
A\in\text{CBio}
an
A\text{-set}
is a pointed set
M\in\text{Set}_{0}
together with an
A\text{-action}
: for
n\geq 1
we have maps

\begin{array}[H]{l}A^{-}(n)\times M^{n}\times A^{+}(n)\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}M\\
\\
(b,(m_{j}),d)\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}\left<b,(m_{j})d\right>_{n}\end{array}

These maps are required to satisfy the following axioms:

zero

\left<b,(m_{j}),d\right>_{n}=\left<b\circ_{j_{0}}0,(m_{j})_{j\not=j_{0}},0\circ_{j_{0}}d\right>_{n-1}
if
m_{j_{0}}=0
.

S_{n}
-variance

\left<b,(m_{j}),d\right>_{n}=\left<b\sigma,(m_{\sigma(j)}),\sigma^{-1}d\right>_{n}

Associativity

For
b\in A^{-}(n)
,
d\in A^{+}(n)

b_{i}\in A^{-}(k_{i}),d_{i}\in A^{+}(k_{i})
,
m=(\overline{m}_{i})\in M^{\sum k_{i}}
,

\left<b\circ(b_{i}),m,(d_{i})\circ d\right>_{\sum k_{i}}=\left<b,\left<b_{i},\overline{m}_{i},d_{i}\right>_{k_{i}},d\right>_{n}

Unit:

\left<1,m,1\right>_{1}=m

Distributivity:

For
b\in A^{-}(n)
,

d\in A^{+}(\ell),

a\in A^{\pm}(|n-\ell|+1)
,

m=(m_{i})\in M^{\min\left\{m,\ell\right\}}
,

\tilde{m}=(m_{1},\cdots m_{i-1}\underbrace{m_{i}\cdots m_{i}}_{|n-\ell|+1},\cdots)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\left<b\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,_{i}a,m,d\right>_{\ell}=\left<b,\tilde{m},a\circ_{i}d\right>_{n},\quad n\geq\ell\\
\\
\left<b,m,a\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,_{i}d\right>_{n}=\left<b\circ_{i}a,\tilde{m},d\right>_{\ell},\quad n\leq\ell\end{array}

For
a\in A(1)
,
m\in M
, we write

a\cdot m:=\left<a,m,1\right>_{1}=\left<1,m,a\right>_{1}

giving an action of the monoid
A(1)
on
M
.

A map
\varphi:M\to N
of
A\text{-Set}
is a set map
\varphi\in\text{Set}_{0}(M,N)
, preserving the
A\text{-action}

\varphi\left(\left<b,(m_{j}),d\right>_{n}\right)=\left<b,\left(\varphi(m_{j})\right),d\right>_{n}

Thus we have a category
A
-Set.

Example of
A
-Sets

(25.2)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\text{Given $\varphi\in\text{CBio}\left(A,B\right)$, the sets $B^{-}(m)$ and $B^{+}(m)$ are
$A$-Sets}\\
\\
\left<b,(x_{i}),d\right>_{n}:=\varphi(b)\circ(x_{i})\circ\sigma_{n,m}\circ\left(\underbrace{\varphi(d),\cdots,\varphi(d)}_{m}\right)\quad,\quad x_{i}\in B^{-}(m).\end{array}

(25.3)

A sub-
A
-set of
A(1)
is just an ideal.

(25.4)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\text{For $A=\mathbb{F}$ we have $\mathbb{F}\text{-Set}\equiv\text{Set}_{0}$ and for
$M\in\text{Set}_{0}$ there is a unique $\mathbb{F}$-action:}\\
\\
\left<b,m_{j},d\right>_{n}=\begin{cases}m_{i}&b=\delta_{i},\;d=\delta_{i}^{t}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\end{array}

(25.5)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\text{For a commutative ring $A$ we have $A\text{-Set}\equiv A\text{-mod}$}\\
\text{the category of $A$ modules. }\end{array}

(25.6)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\text{For $A=\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}$ (resp. $\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{C}}$), the sub-$A$-sets of $\mathbb{R}^{n}$
(resp $\mathbb{C}^{n}$)}\\
\text{ are the convex symmetric subsets $M\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}$ (resp
$M\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{n}$)}\\
\text{ (since the pointwise product of two vectors in the
$\ell_{2}$-unit}\\
\text{ball is a vector in the $\ell_{1}\text{-unit}$ ball).}\end{array}

The category
A
-Set is complete and co-complete. Inverse limits, and filtered
co-limits are formed in
\text{Set}_{0}
, co-limits and sums are more
complicated. Given a set
V
the free
A
-Set on
V
,
A^{V}
is given by

(25.7)

A^{V}=\coprod\limits_{n\geq 1}A^{-}(n)\times V^{n}\times A^{+}(n)/_{\sim}

where
\sim
is the equivalence relation generated by zero,
S_{n}
-invariance, and
distributivity. The element of
A^{V}
can be written, non-uniquely, as

\left<b,v,d\right>_{n}
,
b\in A^{-}(n)
,
v\in V^{n}
,
d\in A^{+}(n)
. For
M\in A\text{-Set}
, we have

(25.8)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\text{Set}(V,M)\equiv A\text{-Set}(A^{V},M)\\
\varphi\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}\tilde{\varphi}\left(\left<b,v_{j},d\right>_{n}\right)=\left<b,\varphi(v_{j}),d\right>_{n}\end{array}

When
V=\left\{v_{0}\right\}
is a singleton
A^{\left\{v_{0}\right\}}\equiv A(1)\cdot v_{0}
, and the free
A
-Set on one generator is just
A(1)

Given a homomorphism
\varphi\in\text{CBio}(B,A)
, we have an adjunction (we use
“geometric” notation):

(25.9)

A
-set
B
-set
\varphi_{*}
\varphi^{*}

where
\varphi_{*}N\equiv N
with the
B
-action
\langle b,m_{j},b^{\prime}\rangle_{n}:=\langle\varphi(b),m_{j},\varphi(b^{\prime})\rangle_{n}
.

The left adjoint is

\varphi^{*}M=\left(\coprod\limits_{n}A_{n}\times M^{n}\times A_{n}\right)\big/\sim
, where
\sim
is the equivalence relation generated by
zero,
S_{n}
-invariance, distributivity, and
B
-linearity:

(25.10)

\langle a,\langle b_{j},m_{i},b^{\prime}_{j}\rangle,a^{\prime}\rangle_{n}=\langle a\circ\varphi(b),m,\varphi(b^{\prime})\circ a\rangle_{m}

a\in A^{-}(n)
,
a^{\prime}\in A^{+}(n)
,
b_{j}\in B^{-}(k_{j})
,
b_{j}^{\prime}\in B^{+}(k_{j})
,
m=\overline{m}_{1},\cdots,\overline{m}_{n}\in M^{\sum k_{i}}
.

In particular, for
B=\mathbb{F}
and
\phi\in\text{CBio}(\mathbb{F},A)
the unique homomorphism,
\phi_{*}
is just
the functor forgetting the
A
-action, and for
V\in\text{Set}_{0}
,

\phi^{*}V=A^{V\setminus\langle 0\rangle}
is the free

A
-set on
V\setminus\left\{0\right\}
.

More generally, for a simplicial distributive bio
A=(A_{n})_{n\geq 0}\in s\text{CBio}\equiv(\text{CBio})^{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta^{\text{op}}}
we have the category of
simplicial
A\text{-Sets}
,
A\text{-sSet}
, with objects the pointed simplicial sets

M=(M_{m})_{m\geq 0}
, with compatible
A_{m}-\text{action}
in dimension
m\geq 0
,

(25.11)

\begin{array}[H]{c}A_{m}^{-}(n)\times(M_{m})^{n}\times A_{m}^{+}(n)\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}M_{m}\\
\\
(b,(m_{i}),d)\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\left<b,(m_{i}),d\right>_{n}\end{array}

and for
\ell\in\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta(m,m^{\prime})

(25.12)

\ell^{\ast}\left(\left<b,(m_{i}),d\right>_{n}\right)=\left<\ell^{\ast}(b),(\ell^{\ast}m_{i}),\ell^{\ast}(d)\right>_{n}

The category
A\text{-sSet}
has a simplicial, cellular, Quillen model structure given by

(25.13)

\begin{array}[h]{l}\begin{array}[h]{lll}(i)\text{ \lx@text@underline{Fibrations}: }&\mathcal{F}_{A}\equiv\phi_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{F}}),&\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{F}}\equiv\text{Kan fibrations.}\\
\\
(ii)\text{ \lx@text@underline{Weak-equivalence}: }&\mathcal{W}_{A}\equiv\phi_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathbb{F}}),&\mathcal{W}_{\mathbb{F}}\equiv\hskip-2.84544pt\left[\hskip-2.84544pt\begin{array}[H]{l}\parbox{56.9055pt}{Weak-equivalence of (pointed) simplicial sets}\end{array}\hskip-7.11317pt\right]\\
\\
(iii)\text{ \lx@text@underline{Cofibrations}: }&\mathcal{C}_{A}\equiv\phantom{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{W}_{A}\cap\mathcal{F}_{A}),\\
\end{array}\\
\hskip 56.9055pt\parbox[t]{196.32384pt}{the maps satisfying the left lifting property
with respect to the trivial fibrations.}\end{array}

The cofibrations can also be characterized as the retracts of the
free
maps
, where a map
f:M_{.}\to N_{.}
is
free
if there exists subsets

V_{n}\subseteq N_{n}
with
\ell^{*}(V_{n^{\prime}})\subseteq V_{n}
for all surjective

\ell\in\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta(n,n^{\prime})
, and such that
f_{n}
induces isomorphism

(25.14)

M_{n}\coprod\phi_{A}^{*}(V_{n})\xrightarrow{\sim}N_{n}

where
\phi_{A}^{*}(V_{n})
is the free
A
-Set
on
V_{n}
.

This model structure is cofibrantly generated

(25.15)

\begin{array}[H]{ll}(i)&\mathcal{F}_{A}\equiv\left\{\phi_{A}^{*}(\Lambda_{n+}^{k})\to\phi_{A}^{*}\big(\Delta(n)_{+}\big)\right\}^{\perp}_{0\leq k\leq n>0}\\
\\
(ii)&\mathcal{W}_{A}\cap\mathcal{F}_{A}\equiv\left\{\phi_{A}^{*}(\partial\Delta(n)_{+})\to\phi_{A}^{*}\left(\Delta(n)_{+}\right)\right\}^{\perp}_{n>0}\end{array}

26
Commutativity

Definition 26.1
.

For
A\in\text{CBio}
,
M\in A\text{-set}
will be called
commutative
if for

b\in A^{-}(n)
,
d\in A^{+}(n)
,
b^{\prime}\in A^{-}(k)
,
d^{\prime}\in A^{+}(k)
,
m=(m_{ij})\in M^{n\times k}
, we have

interchange:

(26.2)

\big<b\circ\underbrace{(b^{\prime}\cdots b^{\prime})}_{n}\sigma_{n,k},m,\underbrace{(d^{\prime},\cdots,d^{\prime})}_{n}\circ d\big>_{n\cdot k}=\big<b^{\prime}\circ\underbrace{(b,\cdots,b)}_{k},m,\sigma_{n,k}\underbrace{(d,\cdots,d)}_{k}\circ d\big>_{n\cdot k}

We let
CA\text{-Set}\subseteq A\text{-Set}
(resp.
CA\text{-sSet}\subseteq A\text{-sSet}
) denote the full subcategory of commutative
A-Sets

(resp. in each dimension
n\geq 0
).

Definition 26.3
.

We say
A\in\text{CBio}
is
n
-commutative
if the sets
A^{-}(n)
and

A^{+}(n)
are commutative, and we let
C_{n}\text{Bio}\subseteq\text{CBio}

denote the full subcategory of
n
-commutative distributive bios. We let

C_{\infty}\text{Bio}=\bigcap\limits_{n\geq 1}C_{n}\text{Bio}
. We say
A
is
totally-commutative if for
b\in A^{-}(n)
,
b^{\prime}\in A^{-}(k)
(resp.

b\in A^{+}(n)
,
b^{\prime}\in A^{+}(k)
), we have

b\circ\underbrace{\left(b^{\prime},\cdots,b^{\prime}\right)}_{n}\sigma_{n,k}=b^{\prime}\circ\underbrace{(b,\cdots,b)}_{k}\qquad\text{resp.}\;(b^{\prime}\cdots b^{\prime})\circ b=\sigma_{n,k}(b,\cdots,b)\circ b^{\prime}.

We let
C_{\text{tot}}\text{Bio}\subseteq C_{\infty}\text{Bio}
denote the full
subcategory of totally-commutative bios; note that
C\text{Rig}\subseteq C_{\text{tot}}\text{Bio}
, and so also
\mathbb{F}
,
\mathbb{Z}_{R}
,

\mathbb{Z}_{C}\in C_{\text{tot}}\text{Bio}
.

For
A\in C_{\text{tot}}\text{Bio}
we have
CA\text{-Set}\equiv A\text{-Set}
.

The inclusion
CA\text{-Set}\hookrightarrow A\text{-Set}
has a left adjoint
M\mapsto M_{C}
; in particular, we have the free-commutative-
A\text{-set}
generated by a set

V
,
A_{C}^{V}
.

27
Symmetric monoidal structure

The category
A
-Set (resp.
A\text{-sSet}
) has a symmetric monoidal structure.

We only describe the
symmetric monoidal structure on
CA\text{-Set}
(resp.
CA\text{-sSet}
), which is closed.

For
M,N,K\in CA\text{-set}
, let the “bilinear maps” be defined by

(27.1)

\text{Bil}_{A}(M,N;K)=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{ll}\varphi:M\wedge N\to K,&\begin{array}[H]{l}\varphi\left(\left<a,m_{j},a^{\prime}\right>,n\right)=\left<a,\varphi\left(m_{j}\wedge n\right),a^{\prime}\right>,\\
\varphi\left(m,\left<a,n_{j},a^{\prime}\right>\right)=\left<a,\varphi\left(m\wedge n_{j}\right),a^{\prime}\right>\end{array}\end{array}\right\}

It is a functor in
K
, and as such it is representable

(27.2)

\text{Bil}_{A}\left(M,N;K\right)\equiv CA\text{-set}\left(M\otimes_{A}N,K\right)

Where
M\otimes_{A}N
is the free-commutative-A-Set on
M\wedge N
modulo the equivalance
relation generated by the
A
-bilinear relations, and where
\otimes:M\wedge N\to M\otimes_{A}N
is the universal
A
-bilinear map. The elements of
M\otimes_{A}N
can be
written, non-uniquely, as
\left<a,m_{j}\otimes n_{j},a^{\prime}\right>_{n}
. We thus get a
bi-functor

(27.3)

\text{\phantom{k}}_{\text{\phantom{k}}_{-}}\otimes_{A_{-}}:CA\text{-Set}\times CA\text{-Set}\to CA\text{-Set}

giving a symmetric monoidal structure on
CA\text{-Set}
, with unit
A(1)_{C}
.

This
symmetric monoidal structure on
CA\text{-Set}
is closed,

(27.4)

CA\text{-Set}\left(M\otimes_{A}N,K\right)\equiv CA\text{-Set}\left(M,\text{Hom}_{A}(N,K)\right)

with the internal Hom functor

(27.5)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\text{Hom}_{A}(_{-},_{-}):\left(A\text{-Set}\right)^{\text{op}}\times CA\text{-Set}\to CA\text{-Set}\\
\text{Hom}_{A}(M,N):=A\text{-Set}(M,N)\end{array}

where the
A
-action on
\text{Hom}_{A}(M,N)
is given by

\left<b,\varphi_{j},d\right>_{n}(m):=\left<b,\varphi_{j}(m),d\right>_{n},\quad b\in A^{-}(n),\;d\in A^{+}(n)\quad\varphi_{j}\in\text{Hom}_{A}(M,N)\quad m\in M.

This in itself is a map of
A
-Set
because of commutativity, and all the other
properties: (associativity, unit, distributivity commutativity) follow from their validity
in
N
.

The tensor product commute with extension of scalars: for
\varphi\in\text{CBio}(B,A)
, and for
M,N\in CB\text{-Set}
, we have

(27.6)

\varphi^{\ast}\left(M\otimes_{B}N\right)\cong\varphi^{\ast}(M)\otimes_{A}\varphi^{\ast}(N)\quad,\quad\varphi^{\ast}B(1)_{C}=A(1)_{C}

We have therefore the adjunction formula

(27.7)

\varphi_{\ast}\text{Hom}_{A}\left(\varphi^{\ast}M,N\right)\cong\text{Hom}_{B}\left(M,\varphi_{\ast}N\right)

The tensor product is distributive over sums,

(27.8)

M\otimes_{A}\left(\coprod\limits_{i}N_{i}\right)\cong\coprod_{i}(M\otimes_{A}N_{i}).

and more generally commutes with colimits.

The category
CA\text{-sSet}
inherit a closed symmetric monoidal structure

(27.9)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\text{\phantom{k}}_{-}\otimes_{A-}:\,CA\text{-sSet}\otimes CA\text{-sSet}\to{}CA\text{-sSet}\\
(M\otimes_{A}N)_{n}:=M_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}N_{n}\\
\\
\text{Hom}_{A}(_{-},_{-}):\left(CA\text{-sSet}\right)^{\text{op}}\otimes CA\text{-sSet}\xmapsto{}CA\text{-sSet}\\
\\
\text{Hom}_{A}(M_{\cdot},N_{\cdot})_{n}:=CA\text{-sSet}\left(M_{\cdot}\otimes\phi_{A}^{\ast}\left(\Delta(n)_{+}\right),N_{\cdot}\right)\end{array}

Here
\phi_{A}^{\ast}\left(\Delta(n)_{+}\right)
is the free-
commutative

A
-sSet
on
\Delta(n)
.

The category
CA\text{-sSet}
has a simplicial, cellular, Quillen model structure, exactly
as in (
25.13
); for the free maps, and for the generating (trivial) cofibrations we take sums,
and free objects
\phi^{\ast}_{A}(_{-})
, in the category
CA\text{-sSet}
.

The model structure is compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure:

For
\left\{i_{\cdot}\;:\;N\to N^{\prime}_{\cdot}\right\}
,
\left\{j_{\cdot}\;:\;M_{\cdot}\to M_{\cdot}^{\prime}\right\}
in
\mathcal{C}_{A}
, we have the
push-out-product

(27.10)

i_{\cdot}\boxtimes j_{\cdot}\,:\,\left(N_{\cdot}\otimes_{A}M_{\cdot}^{\prime}\right)\coprod\limits_{N_{\cdot}\otimes_{A}M_{\cdot}}\left(N_{\cdot}^{\prime}\otimes_{A}M_{\cdot}\right)\to N^{\prime}_{\cdot}\otimes_{A}M_{\cdot}^{\prime}

It is also a cofibration in
\mathcal{C}_{A}
, and moreover, if
i_{\cdot}
is in
\mathcal{W}_{A}
, also

i_{\cdot}\boxtimes j_{\cdot}
is in
\mathcal{W}_{A}
.

To see this compatibility of the
monoidal and model structures, we may assume
i_{\cdot}
and
j_{\cdot}
are free

N_{n}\coprod\phi^{\ast}_{A_{n}}(V_{n})\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}{}N^{\prime}_{n},\quad M_{n}\coprod\phi^{\ast}_{A_{n}}\left(W_{n}\right)\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}{}M^{\prime}_{n}

and then

(27.11)

\left(N_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}M_{n}^{\prime}\coprod\limits_{N_{n}\otimes_{A}M_{n}}N_{n}^{\prime}\otimes_{A_{n}}M_{n}\right)\coprod\phi^{\ast}_{A_{n}}(V_{n})\otimes_{A_{n}}\phi^{\ast}_{A_{n}}(W_{n})\xrightarrow{\;\;\sim\;\;}N^{\prime}_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}M^{\prime}_{n}

and since
\phi^{\ast}_{A_{n}}(V_{n})\otimes_{A_{n}}\phi^{\ast}_{A_{n}}(W_{n})\equiv\phi^{\ast}_{A_{n}}(V_{n}\prod W_{n})
, we see that
i_{\cdot}\boxtimes j_{\cdot}
is also
free.

A map
\varphi\in s\text{CBio}(B_{\cdot},A_{\cdot})
induces a Quillen adjunction

(27.12)

CA_{\cdot}
-sSet
CB_{\cdot}
-sSet
\varphi_{*}
\varphi^{*}

When
A
is an ordinary commutative ring, the bio
\mathscr{P}_{A}
is totally commutative, and
we have by the Dold-Kan correspondence

(27.13)

C\mathscr{P}_{A}\text{-sSet}\equiv\mathscr{P}_{A}\text{-sSet}\equiv\left(\mathscr{P}_{A}\text{-Set}\right)^{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta^{\text{op}}}\equiv Ch_{\geq 0}\left(A\text{-mod}\right).

The model structure on
C\mathscr{P}_{A}\text{-sSet}
corresponds to the projective model
structure on
Ch_{\geq 0}\left(A\text{-mod}\right)
, which embeds in the

stable
model structure
Ch_{+}(A\text{-mod})
. For general
A_{\cdot}\in s\text{CBio}
, the model structure on
CA_{\cdot}\text{-sSet}
is not stable, and we shall
stabilize it, preserving the symmetric monoidal structure by using the symmetric spectra
of
[
44
]
,
[
45
]
.

28
Symmetric Sequences

Let
S_{n}
denote the symmetric group on
n
letters, and let

\sum=\coprod\limits_{n\geq 0}S_{n}
denote the category of finite bijection; it is
equivalent to the category
\text{Iso}(\text{Fin})
of bijections of finite sets. The
category of symmetric sequences in
CA\text{-sSet}
is

(28.1)

\sum(A_{\cdot}):=\left(CA_{\cdot}\text{-sSet}\right)^{\sum}\cong\left(CA_{\cdot}\text{-sSet}\right)^{\text{Iso}(\text{Fin})}

It has objects
M^{\cdot}=\left\{M_{\cdot}^{n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}
with

M_{\cdot}^{n}\in\left(CA.\text{-sSet}\right)^{S_{n}}
a simplicial commutative

A_{\cdot}\text{-set}
with an action of
S_{n}
. The category
\sum(A_{\cdot})
is
complete and co-complete.

The category
\sum(A)
has a closed symmetric monoidal structure

(28.2)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\phantom{\;}{}_{-}\otimes_{\sum(A)^{-}}:\sum(A)\times\sum(A)\to\sum(A)\\
\\
\left(M^{\cdot}\otimes_{\sum(A)}N^{\cdot}\right)^{n}:=\coprod\limits_{p+q=n}S_{n}\begin{array}[t]{c}\times\\
\scriptstyle S_{p}\times S_{q}\end{array}\left(M^{p}\otimes_{A}N^{q}\right)\end{array}

Here the induction functor is the left adjoint of the forgetful functor

(28.3)

\left(CA_{\cdot}\text{-sSet}\right)^{S_{n}}\to\left(CA_{\cdot}\text{-sSet}\right)^{S_{p}\times S_{q}}

and is given by

(28.4)

S_{n}\begin{array}[t]{c}\times\\
\scriptstyle S_{p}\times S_{q}\end{array}(M):=\coprod\limits_{S_{n}/S_{p}\times S_{q}}M

Equivalently, writing
M^{\cdot},N^{\cdot}\in\sum(A)
as functor

\text{Iso}(\text{Fin})\to CA\text{-sSet}
we have

(28.5)

\left(M^{\cdot}_{\cdot}\otimes_{\sum(A)}N^{\cdot}_{\cdot}\right)^{n}=\coprod_{n=n_{0}\coprod n_{1}}M^{n_{0}}_{\cdot}\otimes_{A}N^{n_{1}}.

the sum over all decomposition of
n
as a disjoint union of subsets
n_{0},n_{1}\subseteq n
. The unit of this monoidal structure is the symmetric sequence

(28.6)

{\mathbf{1}}_{A}:=\left(A(1)_{C},0,0,0,\cdots\right)

Remark 28.7
.

Note that this monoidal structure is symmetric,

\mathfrak{T}_{M^{\cdot},N^{\cdot}}:M^{\cdot}\otimes_{\sum(A)}N^{\cdot}\cong N^{\cdot}\otimes_{\sum(A)}M^{\cdot}

This symmetry is clear in the formula (
28.5
)
M^{n_{0}}\otimes_{A}N^{n_{1}}\cong N^{n_{1}}\otimes_{A}M^{n_{0}}
but in formula (
28.2
) the symmetry isomorphisms

S_{n}\begin{array}[t]{c}\times\\
\scriptstyle S_{p}\times S_{q}\end{array}\left(M^{p}\otimes_{A}N^{q}\right)\cong S_{n}\begin{array}[t]{c}\times\\
\scriptstyle S_{q}\times S_{p}\end{array}\left(N^{q}\otimes_{A}M^{p}\right)

involves the
(p,q)
-shuffle
\omega_{p,q}\in S_{n}
that conjugates
S_{p}\times S_{q}
to
S_{q}\times S_{p}
.

The internal Hom is given by

(28.8)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\text{Hom}_{\sum(A)}(_{-},_{-}):\sum(A)^{\text{op}}\times\sum(A)\rightarrow\sum(A)\\
\\
\text{Hom}_{\sum(A)}(M^{\cdot},N^{\cdot})^{n}:=\prod_{k\geq 0}\text{Hom}_{A}\left(M^{k},N^{k+n}\right)\\
\\
\sum(A)\left(M^{\cdot}\otimes_{\sum(A)}N^{\cdot},K^{\cdot}\right)\cong\sum(A)\left(M^{\cdot},\text{Hom}_{\sum(A)}(N^{\cdot},K^{\cdot})\right)\end{array}

For a homomorphism
\varphi\in s\text{CBio}(B,A)
, we have adjunction

(28.9)

\sum(A)
\sum(B)
\varphi_{*}
\varphi^{*}

and
\varphi^{*}
is strict-monoidal

(28.10)

\varphi^{*}\left(M^{\cdot}\otimes_{\Sigma(B)}N^{\cdot}\right)\cong\varphi^{*}(M^{\cdot})\otimes_{\Sigma(A)}\varphi^{*}(N^{\cdot}),\quad\varphi^{*}\left({\mathbf{\mathbbm{1}}}_{B}\right)\cong{\mathbf{\mathbbm{1}}}_{A}.

Remark 28.11
.

One can think of the elements of
\sum(A)
as “Fourier-coefficients”, and
associate with
M^{\cdot}\in\sum(A)
the “Fourier-transform” given by the
“analytic” functor

\begin{array}[H]{l}\widehat{M^{\cdot}}\,:\,CA\text{-sSet}\longrightarrow CA_{\cdot}\text{-sSet}\\
\\
\widehat{M}^{\cdot}(X):=\coprod\limits_{n\geq 0}M^{n}\otimes_{A}^{S_{n}}X^{\otimes n}\\
\\
M^{n}\otimes_{A}^{S_{n}}X^{\otimes n}:=M^{n}\otimes_{A}(\underbrace{X_{\cdot}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{\cdot}}_{n})/_{\sigma m\otimes x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n}\sim m\otimes x_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{\sigma(n)}}\end{array}

The Fourier-transform converts “convolution” (
\equiv
the symmetric product in

\Sigma(A)
) to “multiplication”

(28.12)

\begin{array}[H]{lll}\left(M^{\cdot}\otimes_{\Sigma(A)}N^{\cdot}\right)^{\Lambda}(X)&=\coprod\limits_{n\geq 0}\left(M^{\cdot}\otimes_{\Sigma(A)}N^{\cdot}\right)^{n}\otimes_{A}^{S_{n}}X^{\otimes n}\\
\\
&=\coprod\limits_{p,q\geq>0}\left(M^{p}\otimes_{A}N^{q}\right)\begin{array}[H]{c}\scriptstyle\times\\
\scriptstyle S_{p}\times S_{q}\end{array}S_{p+q}\otimes_{A}^{S_{p+q}}X^{\otimes(p+q)}\\
\\
&=\coprod_{p,q\geq 0}M^{p}\otimes_{A}N^{q}\otimes_{A}^{S_{p}\times S_{q}}X^{\otimes(p+q)}\\
\\
&=\left(\coprod\limits_{p\geq 0}M^{p}\otimes_{A}^{S_{p}}X^{\otimes p}\right)\otimes_{A}\left(\coprod\limits_{q\geq 0}N^{q}\otimes_{A}^{S_{q}}X^{\otimes q}\right)\\
\\
&=\widehat{M^{\cdot}}(X)\otimes_{A}\widehat{N^{\cdot}}(X)\end{array}

29
The sphere spectrum

The categogry
\Sigma(\mathbb{F})\equiv(\text{Set}_{0})^{\Sigma_{.}\times\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta^{\text{op}}}
is the
usual category of symmetric sequence of pointed simplicial sets, and in particular contains
the
sphere-spectrum
:

(29.1)

S^{\cdot}_{\mathbb{F}}:=\left\{S^{n}=\underbrace{S^{1}\wedge\dots\wedge S^{1}}_{n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}

with the permutation action of
S_{n}
on
S^{n}
.

The sphere-spectrum is a monoid
object of
\Sigma(\mathbb{F})
, with multiplication

(29.2)

\begin{array}[H]{c}m:S^{\cdot}_{\mathbb{F}}\otimes_{\Sigma(\mathbb{F})}S^{\cdot}_{\mathbb{F}}\to S^{\cdot}_{\mathbb{F}}\\
\\
m(S^{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}S^{m})\equiv m(S^{n}\wedge S^{m})\equiv S^{n+m}\end{array}

Note that it is a
commutative
monoid,

m=m\circ\mathfrak{T}_{{S_{\mathbb{F}}^{\cdot}},S^{\cdot}_{\mathbb{F}}}
, cf. remark
(
28.10
).

The unit is given by the embedding

(29.3)

\varepsilon:{\mathbf{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\mathbb{F}}\equiv\left(\mathbb{F}({\mathbf{1}}),0,0,\dots\right)\equiv\left(S^{0},0,0,\dots\right)\hookrightarrow S^{\cdot}_{\mathbb{F}},\;\text{using}\;\mathbb{F}(1)=\left\{0,1\right\}\equiv S^{0}.

We write
S^{\cdot}_{A}=\phi^{*}_{A}S^{\cdot}_{\mathbb{F}}
for the corresponding
commutative monoid object of
\Sigma(A)
. We let
S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\subseteq\Sigma(A)
denote the sub-category of
S_{A}^{\cdot}
-modules, this is the category of
“symmetric spectra”. It has objects the symmetric sequences
M^{\cdot}=\left\{M^{n}\right\}\in\Sigma(A)
, together with associative unital

S_{A}^{\cdot}
-action
S_{A}^{\cdot}\otimes_{\Sigma(A)}M^{\cdot}\xrightarrow{m}M^{\cdot}
, or equivalently,
associative unital,
S_{p}\times S_{q}\hookrightarrow S_{p+q}
covariant,
action
S^{p}\wedge M^{q}\to M^{p+q}
. The maps in
S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}
are the maps in

\Sigma(A)
that preserve the
S_{A}^{\cdot}
-action. The category
S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}
is
complete and co-complete.

The category
S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}
has a closed symmetric monoidal structure

(29.4)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\_\otimes_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}\_:S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\times S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}\to S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\\
\\
M^{\cdot}\otimes_{S_{A}^{\cdot}}N^{\cdot}:=\text{Cok}\left\{M^{\cdot}\otimes_{\Sigma(A)}S_{A}^{\cdot}\otimes_{\Sigma(A)}N^{\cdot}\overset{\xrightarrow{m\otimes\text{id}_{N^{\cdot}}}}{\xrightarrow[\text{id}_{M^{\cdot}}\otimes m]{}}M^{\cdot}\otimes_{\Sigma(A)}N^{\cdot}\right\}\end{array}

The unit is

(29.5)

S_{A}^{\cdot}:=\left\{\phi_{A}^{*}S^{0},\phi_{A}^{*}S^{1},\ldots,\phi_{A}^{*}S^{n},\ldots\right\}.

The internal
Hom
is given by

(29.6)

\begin{array}[H]{cc}\text{{Hom}}_{S_{A}}(\_,\_):\left(S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}\right)^{\text{op}}\times S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}\to S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}\\
\\
\text{{Hom}}_{S_{A}}(M^{\cdot},N^{\cdot}):=\mbox{Ker}\left\{\text{{Hom}}_{\Sigma(A)}(M^{\cdot},N^{\cdot})\rightrightarrows\text{{Hom}}_{\Sigma(A)}\left(S^{\cdot}_{A}\underset{{\Sigma(A)}}{\otimes}M^{\cdot},N^{\cdot}\right)\right\}.\end{array}

(29.7)

S_{A}\text{-mod}\left(M^{\cdot}\otimes_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}N^{\cdot},K^{\cdot}\right)\equiv S_{A}\text{-mod}\left(M^{\cdot},\text{{Hom}}_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}(N^{\cdot},K^{\cdot})\right)

The category
S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}
is tensored, co-tensored, and enriched over pointed
simplicial sets

(29.8)

s\text{Set}_{0}\equiv\left(\text{Set}_{0}\right)^{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta^{\text{op}}}\equiv\mathbb{F}\text{-sSet}.

The enrichment is given via the mapping space

(29.9)

\left({N^{\cdot}}^{M^{\cdot}}\right)_{n}\equiv S_{A}\text{-mod}\left(M^{\cdot}\otimes_{A}\phi_{A}^{*}\left(\Delta(n)_{+}\right),N^{\cdot}\right)

For
M\in CA\text{-sSet}
, the “frea-
S_{A}
-module of level
n
” on

M_{\cdot}
,

F_{n}(M_{\cdot})
, is the value at
M_{\cdot}
of the left-adjoint
F_{n}
of the
forgetfull functor

(29.10)

\begin{array}[H]{c}CA_{\cdot}\text{-sSet}\longleftarrow\left(CA_{\cdot}\text{-sSet}\right)^{S_{n}}\longleftarrow S_{A}^{\cdot}\text{-mod}\\
\\
F_{n}(M_{\cdot})=S^{\cdot}_{A}\otimes_{\sum(A)}\left(S_{n}\times M_{\cdot}\right)[n]=\left(0,\dots,0,S_{n}\times M_{\cdot},\cdots,S_{n+p}\times_{S_{p}}S^{p}\otimes_{A}M_{\cdot},\cdots\right)\end{array}

We say
M^{\cdot}\in S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}
is
an
\Omega
-spectrum
, or a
fibrant object
M^{\cdot}\in\left(S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\right)_{\mathcal{F}}
if

M^{\cdot}
is levelwise fibrant, and if the adjoint of the action map

(29.11)

m^{1,n}:S_{A}^{1}\otimes_{A}M^{n}\to M^{n+1}

is a weak equivalence

(29.12)

\left(m^{1,n}\right)^{\natural}:M^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}\text{{Hom}}_{A}\left(S_{A}^{1},M^{n+1}\right)=(M^{n+1})^{S^{1}}:=\Omega M^{n+1}

The
stable
model structure on
S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}
is a Bousfield localization
of the projective model structure, having the same cofibrations, but with the fibrant
objects being
\left(S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\right)_{\mathcal{F}}

(29.13)

\begin{array}[H]{ll}\text{\lx@text@underline{Cofibrations}:}&\mathcal{C}_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}=\text{\phantom{\ }}^{\perp}\left\{\mathcal{W}_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}^{\text{lev}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}^{\text{lev}}\right\}\\
\\
\text{\lx@text@underline{Weak equivalences}:}&\mathcal{W}_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{l}f^{\cdot}\in S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\left(M^{\cdot},N^{\cdot}\right),\\
\left(f^{*}:X^{N^{\cdot}}\to X^{M^{\cdot}}\right)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathbb{F}}\\
\text{for all }X^{\cdot}\in\left(S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\right)_{\mathcal{F}}\end{array}\right\}\\
\\
\text{\lx@text@underline{Fibrations}:}&\mathcal{F}_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}=\left\{\mathcal{C}_{{S}^{\cdot}_{A}}\cap\mathcal{W}_{{S}^{\cdot}_{A}}\right\}^{\perp}\end{array}

It is left proper, simplicial, cellular, Quillen Model structure compatible with its
symmetric monoidal structure. It is moreover
stable
. We let

(29.14)

\mathbb{D}(A)=\text{Ho}\left(S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\right)\cong S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\left[\mathcal{W}_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}^{-1}\right]

denote the associated homotopy category, this is the
derived
category of

A
-Set
. It is a triangulated symmetric monoidal category.

Given a homomorphism
\varphi\in s\text{CBio}(B_{\cdot},A_{\cdot})
we get an induce
adjunction

(29.15)

\mathbb{D}(A)
\mathbb{D}(B)
\mathbb{R}\varphi_{*}
\mathbb{L}\varphi^{*}

and
\mathbb{L}\varphi^{\ast}
is a monoidal functor. We have the adjunction formula
for
M^{\cdot}\in\mathbb{D}(B)
,
N^{\cdot}\in\mathbb{D}(A)
,

(29.16)

\mathbb{R}\varphi_{*}\left(\mathbb{R}\text{Hom}_{S^{\cdot}_{A}}\left(\mathbb{L}\varphi^{\ast}M^{\cdot},N^{\cdot}\right)\right)\equiv\mathbb{R}\text{Hom}_{S^{\cdot}_{B}}\left(M^{\cdot},\mathbb{R}\varphi_{*}N^{\cdot}\right)

30
Quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-modules

Definition 30.1
.

For
X\in\text{GSch}
, a pre-
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-Set (resp. pre-
C\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-Set}
,
pre-
C\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-sSet}
) is an assignment
M
for each open

\mathscr{U}\subseteq X
of an

\mathscr{O}(\mathscr{U})\text{-Set}\;M(\mathscr{U})
(resp.

C\mathscr{O}_{X}(\mathscr{U})\text{-Set}
,

C\mathscr{O}_{X}(\mathscr{U})\text{-sSet}
), and for
\mathscr{V}\subset\mathscr{U}
, on

\mathscr{O}_{X}(\mathscr{U})\text{-map}

r_{\mathscr{V},\mathscr{U}}:M(\mathscr{U})\to M(\mathscr{V})
, and
r_{\mathscr{W},\mathscr{V}}\circ r_{\mathscr{V},\mathscr{U}}=r_{\mathscr{W},\mathscr{U}}
,

r_{\mathscr{U},\mathscr{U}}=\text{id}_{M(\mathscr{U})}
. The maps are natural transformation commuting
with the
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-action (resp. and the simplicial action

\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta^{\text{op}}
); We have the full subcategories of sheaves, denoted by omitting the prefix
“pre”. Thus we have full-embeddings and adjunctions (using the left adjoint of
the inclusion of sheaves in pre-sheaves,
M\mapsto M^{\natural}
the shiftication functor)

(30.2)

pre-
C\mathscr{O}_{X}
-Set
pre-
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-Set
pre-
C\mathscr{O}_{X}
-sSet
C\mathscr{O}_{X}

\mathscr{O}_{X}

\scriptstyle(\;)_{c}
\pi_{0}
\scriptstyle(\;)^{\natural}
\pi_{0}
\scriptstyle(\;)_{c}
\scriptstyle(\;)^{\natural}
\scriptstyle(\;)^{\natural}

All these categories are complete and co-complete. Limits and colimits for pre-sheaves
(resp. sheaves) are created sectionwise over open subset (resp. for co-limit of sheaves use
shification). They have a symmetric monoidal structure, but it is closed only in the
commutative case: for pre-sheaves (resp. sheaves) the symmetric monoidal structure denoted

\overset{\sim}{\otimes}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}
(resp.
\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}
) is defined
sctiowise

(30.3)

\left(M\overset{\sim}{\otimes}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}N\right)(\mathscr{U}):=M(\mathscr{U})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}(\mathscr{U})}N(\mathscr{U})\quad,\quad\Big(\text{resp.}\;M\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}N:=\left(M\overset{\sim}{\otimes}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}N\right)^{\natural}\Big).

The internal Hom, defined only in the commutative case, will be denoted by

\text{Hom}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}
, and is defined for pre-sheaves or sheaves, so that

\text{Hom}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(M,M^{\prime})(\mathscr{U})=\mathscr{O}_{U}\text{-set}(M|_{\mathscr{U}},M^{\prime}|_{\mathscr{U}})

is the equalizer

(30.4)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\ker\left\{\prod\limits_{\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathscr{U}}\text{Hom}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}/_{\mathscr{V}}},\;\left(M(\mathscr{V}),M^{\prime}(\mathscr{V})\right)\rightrightarrows\prod\limits_{\mathscr{W}\subseteq\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathscr{U}}\text{Hom}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}(\mathscr{V})}\left(M(\mathscr{V}),M^{\prime}(\mathscr{W})\right)\right\}\\
\\

Given
A\in\text{CBio}
,
S\subseteq A(1)
a multiplicative subset, and
M\in A\text{-Set}
, (commutative or not!), we can localize
M
to obtain
S^{-1}M\in S^{-1}A\text{-Set}
in the usual way:
S^{-1}M:=(M\times S)/{\simeq}
, with the
equivalence relation
\simeq
given by

(30.5)

(m_{1},s_{1})\simeq(m_{2},s_{2})\;\text{if}\;s\cdot s_{2}\cdot m_{1}=s\cdot s_{1}\cdot m_{2}\;\text{for some $s\in S$}.

We denote by
m/s
the equivalence class of
(m,s)
.

The functor
M\mapsto S^{-1}M
commutes with colimits, finite-limits, tensor products and
Hom’s. We obtain the localization functors

(30.6)

C\mathscr{O}_{A}\text{-Set}
\mathscr{O}_{A}\text{-Set}
C\mathscr{O}_{A}\text{-sSet}
CA\text{-Set}
A\text{-Set}
CA\text{-sSet}
M^{\natural}
\mathscr{M}
\mathscr{M}(\text{spec}A)
M
(\;)_{c}
(\;)_{c}
\pi_{0}
\pi_{0}

With the localization functor
M\mapsto M^{\natural}
given by

(30.7)

M^{\natural}(\mathscr{U}):=\left\{\sigma\;:\;\mathscr{U}\to\coprod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathscr{U}}S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}M,\;\sigma(\mathfrak{p})\in S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}M,\;\sigma\;\text{locally constant}\right\}

where
\sigma
is
locally constant
if for all
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathscr{U}
, there is
neighbourhood
\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)\subseteq\mathscr{U}
, and
m\in M
such that for
q\in D(s),\sigma(q)\equiv m/s\in S_{q}^{-1}M
.

We have an identification of the stalk of
M^{\natural}
at
\mathfrak{p}\in\text{spec}(A)
,

(30.8)

M^{\natural}\Big|_{\mathfrak{p}}=\lim\limits_{\xrightarrow[\mathscr{U}\ni\mathfrak{p}]{}}M^{\natural}(\mathscr{U})\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}S^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}M:=M_{\mathfrak{p}},\qquad\left(\sigma,\mathscr{U}\right)/\sim\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt\;}\sigma(\mathfrak{p})

Moreover, we have the identification of the global sections of
M^{\natural}
over an affine basic
open subset
D(s)\subseteq\text{spec}(A)
,

(30.9)

\begin{array}[H]{rl}\Psi:M\left[1/s\right]:=\left\{s^{\mathbbm{N}}\right\}^{-1}M&\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}M^{\natural}\left(D(s)\right)\\
\frac{m}{s^{n}}&\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt\;}\sigma(\mathfrak{p})\equiv\frac{m}{s^{n}}\in S^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}M\end{array}

(the proof that
\Psi
is a bijection is a repetition of the proof of Theorem (
3
)).

Given a generalized scheme
X
, we have the full subcategory of “quasi-coherent” sheaves of

\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-Sets}
(resp.
C\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-Set}
),
where an object
M
is quasi-coherent if there is a covering of
X
by open affines

X=\cup_{i}\text{spec}(A_{i})
, and there are
M_{i}\in A_{i}\text{-Set}

(resp.
CA_{i}\text{-Set}
), with

M\big|_{\text{spec}(A_{i})}\cong M_{i}^{\natural}
. Equivalently, for all open affine

\text{spec}(A)\subseteq X
, we have

(30.10)

M\big|_{{\text{spec}}(A)}\cong M\left(\text{spec}(A)\right)^{\natural}.

We denote the subcategories of quasi-coherent sheaves by the prefix “q.c.”, so that for
affine scheme
X=\text{spec}(A)
, q.c.
\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-Set}\cong A\text{-Set}
.

The subcategories of quasi-coherent sheaves are closed under colimits and finite-limits in
the associated categories of sheaves, and are therefore co-complete and finite-complete.
Moreover they are closed under the symmetric monoidal structure, so that for
quasi-coherent (resp. commutative)
\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-Sets}

M,N
, and for any affine
open subset
\mathscr{U}=\text{spec}(A)\subseteq X
we have

(30.11)

\begin{array}[H]{c}M\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}N\big|_{\mathscr{U}}\cong\left(M(\mathscr{U})\otimes_{A}N(\mathscr{U})\right)^{\natural}\\
\\
\text{resp. }\;\text{Hom}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\left(M,N\right)\big|_{\mathscr{U}}\cong\text{Hom}_{A}\left(M(\mathscr{U}),N(\mathscr{U})\right)^{\natural}\end{array}

For a mapping of generalized schemes
f\in\text{GSch}(X,Y)
, the functor

f^{\ast}
takes quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{Y}\text{-sets}
to quasi-coherent

\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-sets}
.
Moreover, for affine open subsets
U=\text{spec}(A)\subseteq X
,

\mathcal{V}=\text{spec}(B)\subseteq Y
, with
f(\mathscr{U})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
and with

\varphi=f^{\#}:B\to A
the associated homomorphism, we have for quasi-coherent
sheaf
M
on
Y
,

(30.12)

f^{*}M\Big|_{\mathscr{U}}\cong\left(\varphi^{*}M(\mathscr{U})\right)^{\natural}.

Definition 30.13
.

A mapping of generalized schemes
f:X\to Y
is called a

c\text{-map}

(“compact and quasi-separated”) if we can cover
Y
by open affine
subsets (or equivalently, if for all open affine)

\mathscr{U}=\text{spec}(B)\subseteq Y
, we have that

f^{-1}(\mathscr{U})
is compact, so that

f^{-1}(\mathscr{U})=\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{V}_{i},\qquad\mathcal{V}_{i}=\text{spec}(A_{i}),

is a
finite
union of open affine subsets, and if moreover for

i,j\leq N
, we have
\mathcal{V}_{i}\cap\mathcal{V}_{j}
also compact, so that

\mathcal{V}_{i}\cap\mathcal{V}_{j}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{N_{ij}}\mathcal{W}_{ijk},\qquad\mathcal{W}_{ijk}=\text{spec}\left(A_{ijk}\right).

For a
c\text{-map}

f:X\to Y
, the functor
f_{*}
takes quasi-coherent
sheaves to quasi-coherent sheaves, so we get adjuctions

(30.14)

q.c.
C\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-sSet}
q.c.
C\mathscr{O}_{Y}\text{-sSet}
f^{\ast}
f_{\ast}
q.c.
C\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-Set}
q.c.
C\mathscr{O}_{Y}\text{-Set}
f^{\ast}
f_{\ast}
q.c.
\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-Set}
q.c.
\mathscr{O}_{Y}\text{-Set}
f^{\ast}
f_{\ast}

Indeed, for a quasi-coherent sheaf
M
on
X
, we have
(using the above notations) that

f_{*}M|_{\mathscr{U}}=f_{*}M|_{\text{spec}(B)}
is the equalizer

(30.15)

\begin{array}[h]{l}f_{*}M\big|_{\mathscr{U}}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N}f_{*}\left(M\big|_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\right)\hbox to20.32pt{\vbox to23.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-8.33583pt\lower-9.02356pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

are quasi-coherent.

The adjunctions (
30.14
) satisfy the usual properties, such as the existence of canonical equivalences

(30.16)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\left(g\circ f\right)_{*}\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}g_{*}\circ f_{*}\qquad\left(g\circ f\right)^{*}\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}f^{*}\circ g^{*}\\
\\
f^{*}\left(M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}N\right)\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}f^{*}M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}f^{*}N\\
\\
f_{*}\text{{Hom}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\left(f^{*}M,N\right)\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\text{{Hom}}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\left(M,f_{*}N\right).\end{array}

31
The global derived category

The language of model categories is notoriously difficult for categories of sheaves, and
to describe the derived category of
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-modules one usually uses the
“hairy” injective resolutions (rather than the straightforward projective or even free
resolutions). Using the language of
\infty
categories one can overcome these
difficulties and
define

\mathbb{D}(X)
, the derived category of quasi-coherent

\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-modules}
, as the homotopy category
\mathbb{D}(X)=\text{Ho}D(X)_{\infty}
, of the
\infty
categorical limit
D(X)_{\infty}
of the
\infty

categories
D(A)_{\infty}
of fibrant co-fibrant
S_{A}
-modules, the limit
taken over the category

\text{Aff}/_{X}=\left\{\text{spec}(A)\subseteq X\;\text{open}\right\}

We can do the same for a generalized scheme
X\in\text{GSch}
, and define the derived
category of quasi-coherent commutative
\mathscr{O}_{X}\text{-sets}

\mathbb{D}(X)=\pi_{0}D(X)_{\infty}\quad,\quad D(X)_{\infty}=\infty\mathop{\underleftarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{\text{Aff}/_{X}}D(A)_{\infty}

Using Lurie’s straightening/ unstraightening functors
[
60
]
, corollary 3.3.3.2, this
limit
D(X)_{\infty}
has an equivalent, and more concrete description as the cartesian
sections of an inner, Cartesian and co-Cartesian, fibrantion of
\infty
categories

(31.1)

\begin{array}[h]{l}N_{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta}(\rho):N_{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta}\left(\mathscr{D}(X)\right)\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow N(\text{Aff}/_{X}),\\
\\
\rho:\mathscr{D}(X)=\text{Aff}/_{X}\ltimes\left(S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}\right)\to\text{Aff}/_{X}\;\;\;\text{the forgetful functor.}\end{array}

Here for a generalized scheme
X
,

\mathcal{D}(X)=\text{Aff}/X\ltimes(S^{\cdot}\text{-mod})
is the simplicial
category with objects pairs
(\text{spec}(A),M^{\cdot})
, with

\text{spec}(A)\subseteq X
open affine, and
M^{.}\in S^{\cdot}_{A}\text{-mod}
. The maps are given by

(31.2)

\mathcal{D}(X)\left((\text{spec}(A_{0}),M_{0}^{\cdot}),(\text{spec}(A_{1}),M_{1}^{\cdot})\right)=S_{A_{0}}\text{-mod}\left(\mathbb{L}\phi^{*}M^{\cdot}_{1},M^{\cdot}_{0}\right)

where

\phi:\text{spec}(A_{0})\xhookrightarrow{\;\;}\text{spec}(A_{1})

denotes the inclusion (and are empty if
\text{spec}\left(A_{0}\right)\not\subseteq\text{spec}(A_{1})
).
The coherent nerve of
\mathcal{D}(X)
is the simplicial set

(31.3)

N_{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta}\left(\mathcal{D}(X)\right)_{n}=\text{Cat}_{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta}\left(\mathcal{C}(\Delta^{n}),\mathcal{D}(X)\right)

Explicitly, its elements are given by the data of

\begin{array}[H]{lll}\text{(i)}&(U_{0}\subseteq U_{1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq U_{n})\in N_{n}\left(\text{Aff}/X\right),\quad U_{i}=\text{spec}(A_{i})\subseteq X\text{ open},\\
\\
\text{(ii)}&M^{\cdot}_{i}\in S^{\cdot}_{A_{i}}\text{-mod}\\
\\
\text{(iii)}&\text{For $0\leq i<j\leq n$},\quad\varphi_{ij}\in S^{\cdot}_{A_{i}}\text{-mod}\left(\mathbb{L}\phi_{ij}^{*}M^{\cdot}_{j},M_{i}^{\cdot}\right)\end{array}

where
\phi_{ij}:U_{i}\hookrightarrow U_{j}
denotes the inclusion, and

higher coherent homotopies
given by

(31.4)

\begin{array}[h]{l}\varphi_{\tau}\in S^{\cdot}_{A_{i}}\text{-mod}\left(\mathbb{L}\phi^{*}_{ij}M^{\cdot}_{j}\otimes\Delta(\ell)_{+},M_{i}^{\cdot}\right)\\
\\
\ \qquad\text{for all
$\tau=(\tau_{0}\subseteq\tau_{1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq\tau_{\ell})\in N_{\ell}(\mathcal{P}_{ij})$}\end{array}

(Where
P_{ij}
is the set of subsets of
[i,j]
containing the end
points).

These are required to satisfy:

(31.5)

\displaystyle d_{k}\varphi_{\tau}=\varphi_{(\tau_{0}\subseteq\dots\hat{\tau}_{k}\ldots\subseteq\tau_{\ell})}

and

(31.6)

\begin{array}[h]{l}\text{for $\ell=0$, $\tau=(\tau_{0})=\left\{i,k_{1},\dots,k_{q},j\right\}$},\\
\\
\varphi_{\tau_{0}}=\varphi_{ik_{1}}\circ\mathbb{L}\phi_{ik_{1}}^{*}\left(\varphi_{k_{1},k_{2}}\circ\mathbb{L}\phi_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{*}\left(\ldots\varphi_{k_{q-1},k_{q}}\circ\mathbb{L}\phi_{k_{q-1},k_{q}}^{*}\left(\varphi_{k_{q},j}\right)\dots\right)\right)\end{array}

\begin{array}[h]{ll}\text{A section }m:N\left(\text{Aff}/X\right)\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}N_{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta}\left(\mathcal{D}(X)\right)\;\;,\quad N_{\Delta\mkern-12.0mu\Delta}(\rho)\circ m=\text{id}\end{array}

is
Cartesian
iff for all
n
-simplex

(U_{0}\subseteq U_{1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq U_{n})\in N_{n}\left(\text{Aff}/X\right)
,

U_{i}=\text{spec}(A_{i})
, the data
m(U_{\cdot})
satisfies that all the arrows

\varphi_{ij}\in S^{\cdot}_{A_{i}}\text{-mod}(\mathbb{L}\phi^{*}_{ij}M_{j}^{\cdot},M_{i}^{\cdot})
,
0\leq i<j\leq n
, are weak-equivalences.

The category
D(X)_{\infty}
is symmetric monoidal stable quasi-category, so

\mathbb{D}(X)
is symmetric monoidal triangulated category. Where
X
is an ordinary
scheme,
\mathbb{D}(X)
is equivalent to the usual derived category of quasi-coherent

\mathscr{O}_{X}
-modules. A
c\text{-map}\;f\in\text{GSch}(X,Y)
induces adjunction

f_{\ast}:\mathbb{D}(X)\rightleftarrows\mathbb{D}(Y):f^{\ast}
satisfying the derived
version of (
30.16
).
